产吲哚 3-丙酸细菌 产孢梭菌可预防流感

The indole-3-propionic acid-producing bacteria Clostridium sporogenes protects against influenza
作者： Séverine Heumel, F Giraud, Clément Bordas, Clara Valentin, Lucie Deruyter, Audrey Tarricone, Patrícia Brito Rodrigues, Fabrice Anizon, Pascale Moreau, Sandrine Belouzard
摘要中文翻译摘要 肠道微生物群,特别是细菌代谢产物,在保护肺部免受如甲型流感病毒(IAV)等呼吸道感染方面起着关键作用。然而,IAV感染可能会破坏肠道微生物群,从而可能增加疾病严重程度。值得注意的是,微生物色氨酸代谢产物吲哚-3-丙酸(IPA)水平降低会损害病毒控制并促进肺部炎症,从而加剧IAV感染。在本研究中,我们探讨了Clostridium sporogenes(一种人类厌氧共生菌,能够产生IPA,此前已被证明可以缓解代谢、炎症和神经性疾病)在呼吸道感染中的潜在保护作用。与未治疗的小鼠相比,补充C. sporogenes与较轻的疾病、较低的肺部病毒载量和较少的肺部炎症相关。C. sporogenes的给药调节了感染引起的肠道菌群失调,并恢复了系统性IPA水平,这些水平与临床评分降低和肺部病毒载量减少呈正相关。在基于细胞的实验中,IPA及其类似物未能直接产生抗病毒药物效应,这表明其对免疫系统或其他宿主-病原体相互作用的间接影响。已知IPA可激活的孕烷X受体(PXR)并未参与C. sporogenes介导的保护作用。最后,给药灭活的C. sporogenes改善了流感结果,这种效果也与系统性IPA水平相关。综上所述,这些发现表明C. sporogenes可能代表一种有前景的下一代益生菌候选物,用于预防或缓解急性病毒性呼吸道感染。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在探讨Clostridium sporogenes(一种人类厌氧共生菌,能够产生吲哚-3-丙酸(IPA))在流感A病毒(IAV)感染中的潜在保护作用,鉴于肠道微生物群及其代谢产物在呼吸道感染期间对肺部保护的作用。
设计与对象
本研究涉及感染流感A病毒(IAV)并补充Clostridium sporogenes的小鼠。研究设计重点在于评估这种细菌干预对疾病严重程度、病毒载量和肺部炎症的影响。
核心方法
研究人员给小鼠补充Clostridium sporogenes,并评估其对肺部病毒载量、炎症和肠道微生物群组成的影响。他们还测试了IPA及其类似物在基于细胞的实验中的直接抗病毒药物效应。
主要结果
与未治疗的小鼠相比,补充Clostridium sporogenes与较轻的疾病、较低的肺部病毒载量和较少的肺部炎症相关。灭活的C. sporogenes的给药也改善了流感结果,这种效果与系统性IPA水平的恢复相关。
解释与创新
这些发现表明,C. sporogenes可能调节肠道菌群失调并增强系统性IPA水平,这些水平与疾病严重程度和病毒载量的降低相关,表明其对流感的保护作用可能通过一种新的间接机制实现。
科研与公卫意义
这些结果突显了C. sporogenes作为预防或缓解急性病毒性呼吸道感染的益生菌候选物的潜力,为针对肠道-肺部相互作用的治疗策略提供了新的途径。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于动物模型,IPA的间接效应在流感结果中的作用仍需在人体研究中进一步验证。由于在基于细胞的实验中未观察到直接抗病毒药物效应,因此证据强度中等。

The indole-3-propionic acid-producing bacteria Clostridium sporogenes protects against influenza

Authors: Séverine Heumel, F Giraud, Clément Bordas, Clara Valentin, Lucie Deruyter, Audrey Tarricone, Patrícia Brito Rodrigues, Fabrice Anizon, Pascale Moreau, Sandrine Belouzard
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Gut microbiota, especially bacterial metabolites, play a key role in protecting the lungs against respiratory infections such as influenza A virus (IAV). However, IAV infection can disrupt the gut microbiota, potentially increasing disease severity. Notably, reduced levels of the microbial tryptophan metabolite indole-3-propionic acid (IPA) impair viral control and promote pulmonary inflammation, thereby exacerbating IAV infection. In the current study, we investigated the potential protective effects of Clostridium sporogenes , a human anaerobic commensal and IPA producer previously shown to mitigate metabolic, inflammatory, and neurological disorders, but whose effects during respiratory infection remain unexplored. Relative to untreated mice, C. sporogenes supplementation was associated with less severe disease, lower pulmonary viral loads, and reduced lung inflammation. sporogenes administration modulated infection-induced gut dysbiosis and restored systemic IPA levels, which were positively correlated with reduced clinical scores and pulmonary viral loads. IPA and its analogues failed to exert direct antiviral effects in cell-based assays, suggesting indirect effects on immunity or other host-pathogen interactions. The pregnane X receptor, a xenosensor known to be activated by IPA, was not involved in the protection mediated by C. Lastly, administration of pasteurized C. sporogenes improved influenza outcomes, an effect that also correlated with IPA levels. Collectively, these findings suggest that C. sporogenes may represent a promising next-generation probiotic candidate for the prevention or mitigation of acute viral respiratory infections.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to investigate the potential protective effects of Clostridium sporogenes, a human anaerobic commensal and indole-3-propionic acid (IPA) producer, against influenza A virus (IAV) infection, given the role of gut microbiota and its metabolites in lung protection during respiratory infections.
Design and population
The study involved mice infected with influenza A virus (IAV) and supplemented with Clostridium sporogenes. The design focused on assessing the impact of this bacterial intervention on disease severity, viral loads, and inflammation in the lungs.
Core methods
The researchers administered Clostridium sporogenes to mice and evaluated its effects on pulmonary viral loads, inflammation, and gut microbiota composition. They also tested the direct antiviral effects of IPA and its analogues in cell-based assays.
Main results
Clostridium sporogenes supplementation was associated with less severe disease, lower pulmonary viral loads, and reduced lung inflammation compared to untreated mice. The administration of pasteurized C. sporogenes also improved influenza outcomes, correlating with restored systemic IPA levels.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings suggest that C. sporogenes may modulate gut dysbiosis and enhance systemic IPA levels, which are linked to reduced disease severity and viral loads, indicating a novel indirect mechanism of protection against influenza.
Scientific and public-health significance
These results highlight the potential of C. sporogenes as a probiotic candidate for the prevention or mitigation of acute viral respiratory infections, offering a new avenue for therapeutic strategies targeting gut-lung interactions.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to animal models, and the indirect effects of IPA on influenza outcomes require further validation in human studies. The evidence is moderate due to the lack of direct antiviral effects observed in cell-based assays.

沙特阿拉伯医疗工作者对流感疫苗接种犹豫及其相关因素的横断面研究,2026

Influenza Vaccine Hesitancy and its Associated Factors among Healthcare Workers in Saudi Arabia: A Cross-Sectional Study, 2026
作者： Ashraf AlBukhari, Rafat Abushanab
摘要中文翻译背景:季节性流感导致显著的发病率和死亡率,医疗工作者(HCWs)感染风险增加,可能促进医疗相关传播。尽管季节性流感疫苗(疫苗接种)在沙特阿拉伯是免费且广泛推广的,但接种率仍不理想。本研究评估了沙特阿拉伯医疗工作者对流感疫苗接种的犹豫情况及其相关因素。方法:通过在线自填问卷对沙特阿拉伯的医疗工作者进行横断面分析研究。调查内容包括社会人口学和职业特征、疫苗接种接种史和意愿、工作场所促进因素、感知障碍以及使用改编版的8项世界卫生组织(WHO)SAGE流感疫苗接种犹豫量表(Cronbach’s alpha=0.91)评估犹豫情况。犹豫评分采用中位数和四分位数范围(IQR)进行总结。使用Mann–Whitney U检验和Kruskal–Wallis检验分析组间差异,并采用多变量广义线性模型确定独立相关因素。结果:共有395名医疗工作者参与。在最近一个流感季节中,疫苗接种率为51.4%,有54.9%的参与者计划在下一个季节接种疫苗。工作场所的可及性和鼓励分别被90.1%和89.9%的参与者报告。最常见的障碍是担心副作用(33.7%)、认为流感是一种轻微疾病(32.2%)以及缺乏时间(28.1%)。中位犹豫评分是32(IQR 26–37)。在未调整分析中,职业与犹豫相关(p=0.042)。在多变量模型中,最近的疫苗接种接种是唯一独立相关因素;接种疫苗的参与者评分比未接种者高5.21分(95% CI 3.82–6.59,p

科研与公卫意义
这些发现可以为提高医疗工作者流感疫苗接种率的针对性干预措施提供信息,特别是通过解决感知障碍并增强工作场所对疫苗接种的支持。
局限与证据强度
由于研究采用横断面设计,无法确定因果关系,因此证据强度中等。研究依赖于自我报告数据,可能存在回忆偏差。

Influenza Vaccine Hesitancy and its Associated Factors among Healthcare Workers in Saudi Arabia: A Cross-Sectional Study, 2026

Authors: Ashraf AlBukhari, Rafat Abushanab
Original abstract (English)
Background: Seasonal influenza causes substantial morbidity and mortality, and healthcare workers (HCWs) are at increased risk of infection and may facilitate healthcare-associated transmission. Although influenza vaccination is freely available and widely promoted in Saudi Arabia, uptake remains suboptimal. This study assessed influenza vaccine hesitancy and its associated factors among HCWs in Saudi Arabia. Methods: An analytical cross-sectional study was conducted using a self-administered online questionnaire distributed to HCWs across Saudi Arabia. The survey assessed sociodemographic and professional characteristics, vaccination history and intentions, workplace facilitators, perceived barriers, and hesitancy using an adapted 8-item WHO SAGE Vaccine Hesitancy Scale (Cronbach’s alpha=0.91). Hesitancy scores were summarized using the median and interquartile range (IQR). Group differences were examined using Mann–Whitney U and Kruskal–Wallis tests, and a multivariable generalized linear model was used to identify independently associated factors. Results: A total of 395 HCWs participated. Influenza vaccine uptake during the most recent season was 51.4%, and 54.9% intended to vaccinate in the upcoming season. Workplace accessibility and encouragement were reported by 90.1% and 89.9%, respectively. The most common barriers were fear of side effects (33.7%), perceiving influenza as a mild illness (32.2%), and lack of time (28.1%). The median hesitancy score was 32 (IQR 26–37). Profession was associated with hesitancy in the unadjusted analysis (p=0.042). In the multivariable model, recent vaccination was the only independently associated factor; vaccinated participants had scores 5.21 points higher than unvaccinated participants (95% CI 3.82–6.59, p
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Seasonal influenza causes substantial morbidity and mortality, and healthcare workers (HCWs) are at increased risk of infection and may facilitate healthcare-associated transmission. Although influenza vaccination is freely available and widely promoted in Saudi Arabia, uptake remains suboptimal.
Design and population
An analytical cross-sectional study was conducted using a self-administered online questionnaire distributed to healthcare workers (HCWs) across Saudi Arabia. A total of 395 HCWs participated.
Core methods
The survey assessed sociodemographic and professional characteristics, vaccination history and intentions, workplace facilitators, perceived barriers, and hesitancy using an adapted 8-item WHO SAGE Vaccine Hesitancy Scale (Cronbach’s alpha=0.91). Hesitancy scores were summarized using the median and interquartile range (IQR). Group differences were examined using Mann–Whitney U and Kruskal–Wallis tests, and a multivariable generalized linear model was used to identify independently associated factors.
Main results
Influenza vaccine uptake during the most recent season was 51.4%, and 54.9% intended to vaccinate in the upcoming season. The most common barriers were fear of side effects (33.7%), perceiving influenza as a mild illness (32.2%), and lack of time (28.1%). The median hesitancy score was 32 (IQR 26–37). Profession was associated with hesitancy in the unadjusted analysis (p=0.042).
Interpretation and novelty
In the multivariable model, recent vaccination was the only independently associated factor; vaccinated participants had scores 5.21 points higher than unvaccinated participants (95% CI 3.82–6.59, p.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform targeted interventions to improve influenza vaccine uptake among healthcare workers, particularly by addressing perceived barriers and enhancing workplace support for vaccination.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its cross-sectional design, which cannot establish causality. The evidence is moderate in strength due to the reliance on self-reported data and the potential for recall bias.

用于H1N1 抗病毒药物活性和Molnupiravir纳米载体应用的NiO/GO纳米复合材料设计:一项结合实验与理论的综合研究

Design of NiO/GO Nanocomposites for H1N1 antiviral activity and Molnupiravir Nanocarrier Applications: A Combined Experimental and Theoretical Study
作者： Ali. S. Hamandi, Sahar Z. Al-Sammarraie, Mustafa H. Amin, Hussein H. Abdulghani, Samah G. Ali, Duha S. Ahmed, Majid S. Jabir
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在设计用于H1N1 抗病毒药物活性的NiO/GO纳米复合材料,并探索其作为药物递送系统纳米载体的潜力。背景强调了针对季节性流感病毒开发有效抗病毒药物材料的必要性。
设计与对象
本研究涉及通过溶胶-凝胶法生产纯NiO纳米颗粒和摩尔比为1:1的NiO/GO纳米复合材料。纳米复合材料在MDCK细胞中被测试其对H1N1流感A病毒的活性。
核心方法
方法包括使用FESEM进行结构和形态表征,通过紫外-可见光谱分析光学性质,利用FTIR确认键合,并通过光致发光测量评估电荷分离。此外,还采用了分子动力学模拟和密度泛函理论(DFT)计算来评估结构稳定性和电子性能。
主要结果
The results showed that GO incorporation enhanced crystallinity, reduced particle agglomeration to 29.8 nm, and promoted dispersion on 2D GO sheets. The band gap narrowed from 3.4 to 3.0 eV, and the adsorption strength was found to be -35.77 kcal mol-1 (-1.55 eV), indicating spontaneous and thermodynamically favorable adsorption. The nanocomposite demonstrated no cytotoxic effect on MDCK cells.
解释与创新
作者将这些发现解释为NiO/GO纳米复合材料作为多功能抗病毒药物材料的潜力证据,其在电子性能、抗病毒药物效果和药物负载能力方面表现出协同增强。这代表了一种新型的抗病毒药物纳米载体方法。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现表明,NiO/GO纳米复合材料可以作为抗病毒药物药物递送系统的有前景平台,为如Molnupiravir等药物提供稳定且有效的吸附机制,以对抗H1N1流感A病毒。
局限与证据强度
本研究仅限于使用MDCK细胞进行体外分析,证据基于结构、光学和计算方法,而没有进行体内测试。由于缺乏临床或生物学验证,研究结果的可信度中等。

Design of NiO/GO Nanocomposites for H1N1 antiviral activity and Molnupiravir Nanocarrier Applications: A Combined Experimental and Theoretical Study

Authors: Ali. S. Hamandi, Sahar Z. Al-Sammarraie, Mustafa H. Amin, Hussein H. Abdulghani, Samah G. Ali, Duha S. Ahmed, Majid S. Jabir
Original abstract (English)
Pure NiO nanoparticles and nanocomposites NiO/GO at molar ratio of 1:1 NiO:GO were produced using Sol-Gel and comprehensively considered. The structurers and Morphology characterizations revealed that GO incorporation enhanced crystallinity (crystallite size about 11-12 nm) by FESEM, reduced particles agglomeration to 29.8 nm and promoted dispersion on 2D GO sheets. UV.Vis analysis showed improved optical properties, evidenced by band gap narrowing E g (3.4 to 3.0 eV) indicative of enhanced electronic coupling. The FTIR spectrum confirmed robust NiO/GO bonding through C=C and C=O stretches and Photoluminescence (PL) intensity decreased, confirming charge separation. Besides, we the hypothesized that NiO/GO nanocomposite results in synergistic enhancement of electronic properties, antiviral efficacy, and drug-loading capability through improved interfacial interactions, charge separation, and stable molecular adsorption, establishing NiO/GO as a promising multifunctional antiviral activity of pure NiO nanostructure and NiO/GO nanocomposite against H1N1 Influenza A virus using MDCK cells was detected. Furthermore, the cytotoxicity test against MDCK cells indicated no cytotoxic effect for either pure NiO nanostructure or the NiO/GO nanocomposite was detected. In the current study, the theory of density functional (DFT) calculated are approved to explore the adsorption the adsorption behavior and electronic properties of Molnupiravir on a NiO-doped graphene oxide (NiO/GO) nanocomposite. The optimized geometries confirmed the structural stability of the pristine materials and the adsorption complex. The adsorption strength was evaluated using the Boys-Bernardi counterpoise method, yielding a BSSE-corrected interaction energy of -35.77 kcal mol -1 (-1.55 eV), indicating spontaneous and thermodynamically favorable adsorption. To further assess the structural stability of the adsorption system under dynamic conditions, the molecular dynamics simulations were conducted through the Forcite module of Materials Studio, where the energy fluctuations demonstrated that the Molnupiravir/NiO/GO complex remained stable throughout the simulation. The computed band gap of approximately 3.00 eV corroborated the retention of the semiconducting character of the NiO/GO nanocomposite, whereas the density of states (DOS) analysis revealed observable electronic-state redistribution and orbital hybridization during adsorption. Additionally, molecular docking of the optimized Molnupriavir/NiO/GO nanocomposite against the target protein 9NWB resulted in a binding affinity of -9.8 kcal.ml -1 , indicating significant binding via hydrophobic and cooperation of hydrogen-bonding interactions inside the advice site. These findings emphasize NiO/GO nanocomposite potential as a promising nanocarrier for applications of antiviral drug-delivery systems by showing that it offers a robust adsorption platform while preserving Molnupiravir biological activity.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to design NiO/GO nanocomposites for H1N1 antiviral activity and explore their potential as nanocarriers for drug delivery systems. The background highlights the need for effective antiviral materials against seasonal influenza viruses.
Design and population
The study involved the production of pure NiO nanoparticles and NiO/GO nanocomposites at a 1:1 molar ratio using the Sol-Gel method. The nanocomposites were tested against the H1N1 Influenza A virus using MDCK cells.
Core methods
The methods included structural and morphological characterization using FESEM, UV-Vis analysis for optical properties, FTIR for bonding confirmation, and photoluminescence measurements for charge separation. Additionally, molecular dynamics simulations and density functional theory (DFT) calculations were employed to assess structural stability and electronic properties.
Main results
The results showed that GO incorporation enhanced crystallinity, reduced particle agglomeration to 29.8 nm, and promoted dispersion on 2D GO sheets. The band gap narrowed from 3.4 to 3.0 eV, and the adsorption strength was found to be -35.77 kcal mol-1 (-1.55 eV), indicating spontaneous and thermodynamically favorable adsorption. The nanocomposite demonstrated no cytotoxic effect on MDCK cells.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted the findings as evidence of the NiO/GO nanocomposite's potential as a multifunctional antiviral material, with synergistic enhancements in electronic properties, antiviral efficacy, and drug-loading capability. This represents a novel approach to antiviral nanocarriers.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that NiO/GO nanocomposites could be used as a promising platform for antiviral drug delivery systems, offering a stable and effective adsorption mechanism for drugs like Molnupiravir against H1N1 Influenza A virus.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to in vitro analysis using MDCK cells, and the evidence is based on structural, optical, and computational methods without in vivo testing. The confidence in the findings is moderate due to the lack of clinical or biological validation.

源于海带的岩藻多糖对流感病毒感染的预防作用:通过树突状细胞激活

Preventive Effect of Cladosiphon okamuranus -Derived Fucoidan against Influenza Virus Infection via Dendritic Cell Activation
作者： Yoshiyuki Miyazaki, Hayato Nakano, Shugo Takeuchi, Hideaki Takeuchi, Toshiro Matsui, Daisuke Tachikawa
摘要中文翻译摘要 岩藻多糖是一种存在于褐藻表面粘液成分中的硫酸化多糖。我们之前发现,源于Cladosiphon okamuranus(C. okamuranus)的岩藻多糖在与多种病原体成分刺激时,能够协同激活先天免疫细胞。因此,在本研究中,评估了岩藻多糖对病毒感染的预防作用。将BALB/c小鼠连续14天通过胃内给药给予30、80或240 mg/kg的C. okamuranus来源的岩藻多糖,随后感染H1N1流感病毒。在对照组小鼠中,感染后第5天症状评分迅速上升,所有小鼠在第9天均死亡,而在给予80 mg/kg或更高剂量岩藻多糖的小鼠中,症状得到缓解,且感染相关死亡被预防。感染后3天内,肺部病毒增殖在80 mg/kg剂量的岩藻多糖作用下显著受到抑制。此外,岩藻多糖诱导了有效的免疫应答,表现为I型和II型干扰素表达的上调以及免疫细胞的聚集。这些结果清楚地表明,岩藻多糖在预防流感感染方面具有显著效果。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在评估岩藻多糖对病毒感染的预防作用,基于之前发现的岩藻多糖在接触病原体成分时能够激活先天免疫细胞的结论。
设计与对象
本研究涉及BALB/c小鼠,这些小鼠在感染H1N1流感病毒前,连续14天通过胃内给药给予30、80或240 mg/kg的C. okamuranus来源的岩藻多糖。
核心方法
小鼠连续14天通过胃内给药给予岩藻多糖,随后感染H1N1流感病毒,并监测症状进展和存活情况。
主要结果
在对照组中,感染后第5天症状评分迅速上升,所有小鼠在第9天均死亡。相比之下,给予80 mg/kg或更高剂量岩藻多糖的小鼠表现出症状缓解,且无感染相关死亡。
解释与创新
结果表明,岩藻多糖能够通过减轻症状和抑制病毒增殖有效预防流感感染,突显了其作为预防剂的潜力。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现表明,岩藻多糖可能成为预防流感感染的有价值的补充手段,特别是在那些面临严重后果风险的人群中。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于动物模型,且证据基于摘要报告,可能无法完全反映人类流感感染的复杂性。证据强度中等。

Preventive Effect of Cladosiphon okamuranus -Derived Fucoidan against Influenza Virus Infection via Dendritic Cell Activation

Authors: Yoshiyuki Miyazaki, Hayato Nakano, Shugo Takeuchi, Hideaki Takeuchi, Toshiro Matsui, Daisuke Tachikawa
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Fucoidan is a sulfated polysaccharide contained in the viscous components covering the surface of brown algae. We previously revealed that fucoidan derived from Cladosiphon okamuranus (C. okamuranus) cooperatively activates innate immune cells upon stimulation with several pathogen components. Therefore, in this study, the preventive effect of fucoidan against viral infection was evaluated. BALB/c mice were continuously administered 30, 80, or 240 mg/kg of C. okamuranus-derived fucoidan intragastrically for 14 days, subsequently infected with the H1N1 influenza virus. In the control mice, the symptom scores rapidly increased from day 5 postinfection, and all mice died by day 9, whereas in the mice administered 80 mg/kg or more of fucoidan, the symptoms were ameliorated, and death associated with infection was prevented. Viral proliferation in the lungs was significantly suppressed with 80 mg/kg fucoidan during the 3 days postinfection. Furthermore, fucoidan induced an effective antiviral response, characterized by the upregulation of type I and II interferon expression and the accumulation of immune cells. These results clearly demonstrate the efficacy of fucoidan in preventing influenza infection.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to evaluate the preventive effect of fucoidan against viral infection, building on prior findings that fucoidan activates innate immune cells when exposed to pathogen components.
Design and population
The study involved BALB/c mice that were administered 30, 80, or 240 mg/kg of C. okamuranus-derived fucoidan intragastrically for 14 days before being infected with the H1N1 influenza virus.
Core methods
The mice were continuously given fucoidan via intragastric administration for 14 days, then infected with the H1N1 influenza virus, and monitored for symptom progression and survival.
Main results
In the control group, symptom scores increased rapidly from day 5 postinfection, and all mice died by day 9. In contrast, mice administered 80 mg/kg or more of fucoidan showed ameliorated symptoms and no death associated with infection.
Interpretation and novelty
The results demonstrate that fucoidan can effectively prevent influenza infection by reducing symptoms and mortality, highlighting its potential as a preventive agent.
Scientific and public-health significance
These findings suggest that fucoidan could be a valuable supplement in preventing influenza infection, particularly in populations at risk of severe outcomes.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to animal models, and the evidence is based on an abstract-only report, which may not fully capture the complexity of human influenza infection. The evidence is moderate in strength.

人类甲型流感 (H5N1) 感染的 抗病毒药物 治疗:治疗 时机、临床结果、病毒学结果和 抗病毒药物 耐药性:系统评价

Antiviral Treatment of Human Influenza A(H5N1) Infection: Treatment Timing, Clinical Outcomes, Virological Outcomes, and Antiviral Resistance: A Systematic Review
作者： Haaly Al Muaafy Ruslan Musanip, Juniastuti Juniastuti, Kaiser Gerald Handoyo, Rizal Rachbini Mahlevi
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述重点关注人类甲型流感(H5N1)感染的 抗病毒药物 治疗,检查 治疗 时机、临床结果、病毒学结果和 抗病毒药物 耐药性。核心问题是 抗病毒药物 治疗 是否可以改善人类 A(H5N1) 感染的结果。
证据基础与方法
从数据库建立到 2026 年 7 月 1 日,对 PubMed、Scopus 和 ScienceDirect 进行了系统检索。使用纽卡斯尔-渥太华量表和 JBI 病例系列清单评估方法学质量。不同研究的临床和方法学特征各不相同;因此,对调查结果进行了叙述性综合。在综合出版物中的研究结果时,考虑了潜在的人群重叠,以避免重复汇总患者数据。
共识与结论
Oseltamivir treatment was associated with a lower hazard of death than no documented anti-influenza antiviral treatment (adjusted hazard ratio 0.51, 95% confidence interval 0.34–0.77). Treatment within 2 days showed lower odds of death, although the overall estimate was not statistically significant (adjusted odds ratio 0.17, 95% confidence interval 0.03–1.04). Among patients without respiratory failure at treatment initiation, earlier treatment was associated with lower mortality (adjusted odds ratio 0.17, 95% confidence interval 0.03–0.89).
争议与缺口
抗病毒药物 有效性的证据受到观察设计、异质 治疗 时间安排和潜在重叠患者群体的限制。由于各研究的临床和方法学特征存在差异,因此对研究结果进行了叙述性综合。因果推断仍然受到混杂、异质性和潜在重叠研究人群的限制。
科研与实践启示
其他 抗病毒药物 的证据有限,并且确定了一个确认的 治疗 出现的 H274Y 神经氨酸酶抗性事件。本系统综述综合了 抗病毒药物 方案、治疗 时机、临床和病毒学结果以及实验室确诊的人类 A(H5N1) 感染中 抗病毒药物 耐药性的证据。

Antiviral Treatment of Human Influenza A(H5N1) Infection: Treatment Timing, Clinical Outcomes, Virological Outcomes, and Antiviral Resistance: A Systematic Review

Authors: Haaly Al Muaafy Ruslan Musanip, Juniastuti Juniastuti, Kaiser Gerald Handoyo, Rizal Rachbini Mahlevi
Original abstract (English)
Human influenza A(H5N1) infection remains a clinically important zoonotic disease, but evidence on antiviral effectiveness is limited by observational designs, heterogeneous treatment timing, and potentially overlapping patient populations. This systematic review synthesized evidence on antiviral regimens, treatment timing, clinical and virological outcomes, and antiviral resistance in laboratory-confirmed human A(H5N1) infection. A systematic search of PubMed, Scopus, and ScienceDirect was conducted from database inception to 1 July 2026. Methodological quality was assessed using the Newcastle–Ottawa Scale and the JBI Case Series Checklist. The clinical and methodological characteristics varied across studies; consequently, the findings were synthesized narratively. Potential population overlap was considered when synthesizing findings across publications to avoid duplicate aggregation of patient data. Of 2,329 records identified, 22 publications were included. Oseltamivir treatment was associated with a lower hazard of death than no documented anti-influenza antiviral treatment (adjusted hazard ratio 0.51, 95% confidence interval 0.34–0.77). Treatment within 2 days showed lower odds of death, although the overall estimate was not statistically significant (adjusted odds ratio 0.17, 95% confidence interval 0.03–1.04). Among patients without respiratory failure at treatment initiation, earlier treatment was associated with lower mortality (adjusted odds ratio 0.17, 95% confidence interval 0.03–0.89). Evidence for other antivirals was limited, and one confirmed treatment-emergent H274Y neuraminidase resistance event was identified. Overall, oseltamivir, particularly when initiated early and before advanced respiratory failure, was associated with improved clinical outcomes, although causal inference remains limited by confounding, heterogeneity, and potentially overlapping study populations.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on antiviral treatment of human influenza A(H5N1) infection, examining treatment timing, clinical outcomes, virological outcomes, and antiviral resistance. The central question is whether antiviral treatment improves outcomes in human A(H5N1) infection.
Evidence base and method
A systematic search of PubMed, Scopus, and ScienceDirect was conducted from database inception to 1 July 2026. Methodological quality was assessed using the Newcastle–Ottawa Scale and the JBI Case Series Checklist. The clinical and methodological characteristics varied across studies; consequently, the findings were synthesized narratively. Potential population overlap was considered when synthesizing findings across publications to avoid duplicate aggregation of patient data.
Consensus and conclusions
Oseltamivir treatment was associated with a lower hazard of death than no documented anti-influenza antiviral treatment (adjusted hazard ratio 0.51, 95% confidence interval 0.34–0.77). Treatment within 2 days showed lower odds of death, although the overall estimate was not statistically significant (adjusted odds ratio 0.17, 95% confidence interval 0.03–1.04). Among patients without respiratory failure at treatment initiation, earlier treatment was associated with lower mortality (adjusted odds ratio 0.17, 95% confidence interval 0.03–0.89).
Controversies and gaps
Evidence on antiviral effectiveness is limited by observational designs, heterogeneous treatment timing, and potentially overlapping patient populations. The findings were synthesized narratively due to variability in clinical and methodological characteristics across studies. Causal inference remains limited by confounding, heterogeneity, and potentially overlapping study populations.
Research and practice implications
Evidence for other antivirals was limited, and one confirmed treatment-emergent H274Y neuraminidase resistance event was identified. This systematic review synthesized evidence on antiviral regimens, treatment timing, clinical and virological outcomes, and antiviral resistance in laboratory-confirmed human A(H5N1) infection.

日本Izumi平原野生鸟类和白鹭栖息地水体中H5N1高致病性禽流感病毒的季节动态及基因型更替,2024/25年

Seasonal Dynamics and Genotype Turnover of H5N1 High-Pathogenicity Avian Influenza Viruses in Wild Birds and Crane Roost Water in the Izumi Plain, Japan, 2024/25

A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

National flu and COVID-19 surveillance report: 12 November 2026 (week 46) - GOV.UK

National flu and COVID-19 surveillance report: 12 November 2026 (week 46) - GOV.UK
新闻简报The National Flu and COVID-19 Surveillance Report for 12 November 2026 (week 46) highlights that influenza activity in England has increased and is circulating at low levels. Emergency department attendances for influenza-like-illness (ILI) have risen above seasonally expected levels, and the number of influenza-confirmed acute respiratory infection (ARI) incidents has increased compared to the previous week. The report also notes that vaccine uptake among certain groups, such as those aged 65 and over, is lower than the previous season. While influenza laboratory surveillance and GP swabbing positivity have transitioned from using the Moving Epidemic Method (MEM) to the Mean Standard Deviation Method (MSD), data for weeks 41 and 42 was incomplete, but this has been corrected since week 43. The report emphasizes that the methods used for defining activity levels are experimental and may be subject to future revision. Additionally, it mentions that the Second Generation Surveillance System (SGSS) captures test results for influenza and other respiratory viruses, and the unified sample dataset (USD) is used to calculate positivity rates for SARS-CoV-2. Overall, the report provides an updated overview of influenza and related respiratory virus activity in England, with a focus on monitoring and data collection methods.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该报告基于2026年第45周(2026年11月2日至11月8日)的数据。
地点与对象
该报告涵盖英格兰,重点关注流感及其他季节性呼吸道病毒在人群中的情况。
事件
该报告指出,流感活动有所增加,且在低水平传播,流感样疾病的急诊部门就诊人数超过季节性预期水平。同时,报告还提到流感确诊的急性呼吸道感染病例数较前一周有所上升。
规模、影响与风险
流感样疾病的急诊部门就诊人数超过季节性预期水平,且流感确诊的急性呼吸道感染病例数较前一周有所上升。某些年龄组和临床高风险群体的疫苗接种率低于上一季节。
应对、状态与不确定性
已发布简报通知,向NHS通报流感活动情况。报告指出,流感实验室监测和GP拭子阳性率已从使用移动流行病学方法(MEM)转为使用均值标准差方法(MSD)。第41和42周的数据不完整,但从第43周起已得到修正。用于定义活动水平的方法是实验性的,可能在未来进行修订。
A high priorityPublic-health news

National flu and COVID-19 surveillance report: 12 November 2026 (week 46) - GOV.UK

News Brief
The National Flu and COVID-19 Surveillance Report for 12 November 2026 (week 46) highlights that influenza activity in England has increased and is circulating at low levels. Emergency department attendances for influenza-like-illness (ILI) have risen above seasonally expected levels, and the number of influenza-confirmed acute respiratory infection (ARI) incidents has increased compared to the previous week. The report also notes that vaccine uptake among certain groups, such as those aged 65 and over, is lower than the previous season. While influenza laboratory surveillance and GP swabbing positivity have transitioned from using the Moving Epidemic Method (MEM) to the Mean Standard Deviation Method (MSD), data for weeks 41 and 42 was incomplete, but this has been corrected since week 43. The report emphasizes that the methods used for defining activity levels are experimental and may be subject to future revision. Additionally, it mentions that the Second Generation Surveillance System (SGSS) captures test results for influenza and other respiratory viruses, and the unified sample dataset (USD) is used to calculate positivity rates for SARS-CoV-2. Overall, the report provides an updated overview of influenza and related respiratory virus activity in England, with a focus on monitoring and data collection methods.
Original source text (English)
This publication is licensed under the terms of the Open Government Licence v3.0 except where otherwise stated. To view this licence, visit nationalarchives.gov.uk/doc/open-government-licence/version/3 or write to the Information Policy Team, The National Archives, Kew, London TW9 4DU, or email: psi@nationalarchives.gov.uk . Where we have identified any third party copyright information you will need to obtain permission from the copyright holders concerned. This publication is available at https://www.gov.uk/government/statistics/national-flu-and-covid-19-surveillance-reports-2026-to-2027-season/national-flu-and-covid-19-surveillance-report-12-november-2026-week-46 This report summarises the information from the surveillance systems which are used to monitor COVID-19 (caused by severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2)), influenza, respiratory syncytial virus ( RSV ) and diseases caused by other seasonal respiratory viruses in England. The report is based on data up to week 45 of 2026 (2 November to 8 November 2026). The main messages of this report are: Annual reports for the 2025 to 2026 season: Influenza in the UK, annual epidemiological report: winter 2025 to 2026 (published 28 May 2026) Epidemiology of COVID-19 in England (published 25 June 2026) Seasonal influenza vaccine uptake in all GP patients: annual report 2025 to 2026 (published 28 May 2026) Seasonal influenza vaccine uptake in school-aged children: annual report 2025 to 2026 (published 28 May 2026) Seasonal influenza vaccine uptake in frontline healthcare workers: annual report 2025 to 2026 (published 28 May 2026) Influenza activity increased and is circulating at low levels. Emergency department ( ED ) attendances for influenza-like-illness ( ILI ) increased and were above seasonally expected levels. The number of influenza-confirmed acute respiratory infection ( ARI ) incidents increased compared with the previous week. A briefing note has been issued to inform the NHS that influenza activity is now above baseline levels. Compared with the equivalent week last season (week 45 data, 2025 to 2026), vaccine uptake is lower among patients aged 65 years and over (63.1% versus 65.1%), patients aged 6 months to under 65 years in clinical risk groups (30.4% versus 31.4%), those aged 2 years (35.8% versus 36.6%), and those aged 3 years (35.6% versus 36.8%). In contrast, vaccine uptake is higher among pregnant women (32.7% versus 31.4%). Influenza vaccination data was unavailable or incomplete at the time of analysis in weeks 41 and 42 and led to several cohorts not being published in week 42. This has been corrected since week 43, and all cohorts are included. This will be corrected retrospectively in the report for week 42. There remains an issue that affects the attribution of vaccinations by provider setting. This is not expected to affect overall vaccination uptake estimates, which are considered to remain an accurate reflection of the influenza vaccination programme. COVID-19 activity was stable and is circulating at low levels. ED attendances for COVID-19-like illness decreased. The number of COVID-19 confirmed acute respiratory infections ( ARI ) incidents increased compared with the previous week. By the end of week 45 2026, 51.8% of all people aged 75 years and over in England had been vaccinated with an autumn 2026 dose. Similarly, 22.1% of all people aged under 75 years with a weakened immune system in England had been vaccinated with an autumn 2026 dose since 1 October 2026. RSV hospital activity increased to low levels with other indicators within baseline levels. ED attendances for acute bronchiolitis increased and were below seasonally expected levels. Note 1: these indicators use the moving epidemic method ( MEM ) and the mean standard deviation method ( MSD ) to define thresholds to determine their respective levels of activity. Further information on these methods can be found in Influenza surveillance in Europe: establishing epidemic thresholds by the Moving Epidemic Method and Setting thresholds to determine COVID-19 activity levels using the mean standard deviation ( MSD ) method, England, 2022 to 2024 . The MEM approach is well-established for some influenza surveillance indicators, however, for other indicators both the MEM and MSD are experimental and may be subject to future revision. Influenza laboratory surveillance and GP swabbing positivity have transitioned from using MEM to using MSD . These approaches will be considered alongside expert opinion and triangulation of other data sources. The Second Generation Surveillance System ( SGSS ) captures test result information for notifiable infectious diseases, including COVID-19 and influenza, from laboratories in England. The unified sample dataset ( USD ) stores all SARS-CoV-2 test results reported to SGSS , Respiratory DataMart and UKHSA laboratories, and is used to calculate the percentage of tests positive for SARS-CoV-2 among all SARS-CoV-2 tests conducted. As of 10 November 2026, there were a total of 1,587 COVID-19 cases identified in hospital settings in week 45, decreasing from 1,914 cases in the previous week. SARS-CoV-2 (COVID-19) PCR positivity in hospital settings remained stable in week 45, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 7.2% up to Sunday 8 November 2026 compared with 7.4% for the same period on the previous Sunday. Positivity rates were highest among those aged 85 years and over with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 10.7% up to Sunday 8 November 2026. This has remained stable compared to 10.7% in the same age group on the previous Sunday. Note 2: data from previous seasons is aligned by day. Note 3: testing policy and practice may change over time which can impact positivity rates, therefore comparisons over time should be interpreted with caution. Notable changes in testing policy occurred during 2022 to 2023, which are outlined in the data quality report . Note 4: the highlighted line corresponds to the age group in the subplot title, grey lines correspond to all other age groups. Influenza positivity remained stable in week 45, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 8.1% up to Sunday 8 November 2026. This is compared with 7.8% on the previous Sunday. By type, influenza A positivity remained stable in week 45, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 7.2% up to Sunday 8 November 2026. Influenza B positivity remained stable in the same week, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 0.3% up to Sunday 8 November 2026. Influenza A positivity rates were highest among those aged between 5 and 11 years, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 12.8% up to Sunday 8 November 2026. This has increased from 10.7% in the same age group on the previous Sunday. Influenza B positivity rates for the same period were highest among those aged between 12 and 17 years, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 1.6%. This has increased from 1.0% in the same age group on the previous Sunday. RSV swab-positivity based PCR test results reported through SGSS have been included as a pilot indicator from week 49 2025 onwards. RSV positivity increased in week 45, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 3.2% up to Sunday 8 November 2026, compared with 2.5% up to the previous Sunday. RSV positivity rates were highest in those aged between 0 and 4 years with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 19.6% up to Sunday 8 November 2026 in week 45. This has increased from 15.6% in the same age group on the previous Sunday. Respiratory DataMart is a sentinel laboratory-based surveillance system where participating laboratories report positive and negative test results for a number of respiratory viruses from samples primarily taken in hospital. A small proportion of primary care samples are also included in this reporting. In week 45, data is based on reporting from 9 out of the 14 sentinel laboratories. In week 45, 4,231 respiratory specimens reported through the Respiratory DataMart System were tested for influenza. There were 328 positive samples for influenza: 216 influenza A (not subtyped), 10 influenza A (H3N2), 88 influenza A (H1N1)pdm09, and 18 influenza B. Overall, influenza positivity remained stable at 7.8% in week 45 compared with 7.5% in the previous week. In week 45, 4,588 respiratory specimens reported through the Respiratory DataMart System were tested for SARS-CoV-2. There were 271 positive samples for SARS-CoV-2. SARS-CoV-2 positivity decreased slightly to 5.9% compared with 7.2% in the previous week, with the highest positivity in those aged 80 years and over at 9.4%. RSV positivity increased slightly to 3.2%, with the highest positivity in those aged under 5 years at 17.5%. Adenovirus positivity remained stable at 1.8%, with the highest positivity in those aged between 5 and 14 years at 5.6%. Human metapneumovirus ( hMPV ) positivity decreased to 0.1%, with the highest positivity in those aged between 65 and 79 years at 0.3%. Parainfluenza positivity remained stable at 3.6%, with the highest positivity in those aged under 5 years at 11.7%. Rhinovirus positivity decreased slightly to 14.1%, with the highest positivity in those aged under 5 years at 28.4%. DataMart data is provisional and subject to retrospective updates. Note 5: shading represents 95% confidence intervals. This section is updated fortnightly. Data below was last updated in the week 45 report. UKHSA conducts genomic surveillance of SARS-CoV-2 lineages. This section provides an overview of circulating lineages in England, derived from data on sequenced PCR -positive SARS-CoV-2 samples in SGSS . The prevalence of UKHSA -designated lineages among sequenced cases is presented in Figure 13. To account for reporting delays, we report the proportion of lineages within COVID-19 cases that have had a sequenced positive sample between 28 September 2026 and 11 October 2026. Note that low sequencing numbers, especially within the latest reporting period, will impact the accuracy of the prevalence estimates. These most recent figures should therefore be interpreted with caution. Note that lineages will be grouped independently from their parent lineage once they reach sufficient prevalence, and may be re-grouped into their parent lineage if their prevalence subsequently falls. The data quality report contains more information on lineage groupings. Analysis of viruses from primary and secondary care shows that circulating A(H1N1)pdm09 viruses show low genetic diversity and all belong to clade D.3.1.1. Observations published from the WHO influenza vaccine composition meeting (VCM) in September 2026 suggest low reactivity of some viruses within the D.3.1.1 subclade with post-infection ferret antisera raised against A/Missouri/11/2025 (H1N1)pdm09-like virus – the A(H1N1)pdm09 component in 2026 Southern and 2026 to 2027 Northern Hemisphere cell- and egg-based influenza vaccines - and human sera post-vaccination with the Southern Hemisphere vaccine 2026. It is not yet clear how vaccine effectiveness against clinical disease may be affected by these observations. Vaccines typically still provide protection against severe clinical disease when drifted strains are seen with only a modest reduction in effectiveness. The characterisation of circulating A(H3N2) viruses shows that A(H3N2) viruses are currently showing low genetic diversity. All sequenced viruses to date belong to genetic subclade K. To date, only a small number of influenza B viruses have been detected, which show some genetic diversity. Between week 35 2026 (week ending 30 August 2026) and week 45 2026 (week ending 8 November 2026), the UKHSA respiratory virus unit ( RVU ) has genetically characterised 397 seasonal influenza viruses, and identified 267 influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 viruses, 96 influenza A(H3N2) viruses, and 34 influenza B viruses. Details of the characterised viruses by subtype are shown in Tables 1, 2 and 3. The RVU has confirmed by genome sequencing the detection of live attenuated influenza vaccine ( LAIV ) viruses in 16 influenza A/B positive samples, collected from children aged between 2 and 16 years. UKHSA RVU performs antigenic characterisation of influenza A(H1N1)pdm09, influenza A(H3N2) and influenza B viruses using haemagglutination inhibition ( HI ) assays. Data from these assays are used to compare how similar the currently circulating influenza viruses are to the strains included in seasonal influenza vaccines, and to monitor for changes in circulating influenza viruses. Similarity of currently circulating influenza strains to vaccine strains is defined as having an antibody titre within 4-fold when compared with reference viruses representative of the vaccine strain. Antigenic characterisation of viruses in the current reporting period is underway. A(H1N1)pdm09: 13 A(H1N1)pdm09 viruses have been antigenically characterised and 11 were similar to reference viruses representative of the A/Missouri/11/2025 (H1N1)pdm09-like Northern Hemisphere 2026 to 2027 (H1N1)pdm09 vaccine strains. A(H3N2): 6 A(H3N2) viruses have been antigenically characterised and 6 were similar to reference viruses representative of the A/Darwin/1454/2025 (H3N2)-like and A/Darwin/1415/2025 (H3N2)-like Northern Hemisphere 2026 to 2027 (H3N2) vaccine strains. Influenza positive samples are screened for mutations in the virus neuraminidase ( NA ) and the cap-dependent endonuclease of the polymerase acidic protein (PA) genes known to confer neuraminidase inhibitor (oseltamivir and zanamivir) or baloxavir resistance, respectively. Results from this surveillance are given in table 4. There have been seven detections with mutations related to reduced susceptibility/resistance to oseltamivir and/or reduced susceptibility to baloxavir. Data is presented on viral ARI incidents in different settings that are reported to UKHSA health protection teams ( HPTs ). Please note that reporting practices are known to vary between seasons and between regions. Any interpretation of temporal and regional trends should consider the likelihood of differences in reporting of ARI incidents over time and between regions. There were 124 new ARI incidents reported in week 45 in England. These included: 114 incidents from care homes, of which 25 were due to SARS-CoV-2, 23 were due to influenza A, 12 were due to influenza (no type information available), 5 were due to multiple pathogens, 4 were due to other pathogens and 1 was due to RSV . No pathogen was reported in 44 incidents 2 incidents from hospitals, of which 2 were due to SARS-CoV-2 7 incidents from educational settings, of which 3 were due to RSV and 1 was due to influenza (no type information available). No pathogen was reported in 3 incidents 1 incident from other settings, of which 1 was due to SARS-CoV-2 FluSurvey is an internet-based participatory surveillance system based on the InfluenzaNet platform. It monitors trends of influenza-like illness ( ILI ) in the community using self-reported respiratory symptoms from registered participants. As a participatory surveillance system, new signups can occur throughout the season and participation can vary in each week. The European Centre for Disease Control ( ECDC ) ILI case definition of sudden onset of symptoms with at least one of fever (chills), malaise, headache, muscle pain and at least one of cough, sore throat, shortness of breath is used for reporting. Please note that ILI is a broad definition and can include other respiratory illnesses such as COVID-19. Healthcare use is presented as self-reported use of health services among participants meeting the ILI ECDC case definition. Where a person reports use of more than one healthcare service, secondary care will be indicated over primary care use and physical attendance to primary care will be indicated over use of remote services (for example, online NHS services, telephoning their GP or 111). During week commencing 2 November 2026: there were 2,484 participants who completed the weekly symptoms questionnaire 361 (14.5%) reported fever or cough and 126 (5.1%) met the ILI case definition, in the past week 14.3% of participants meeting the ILI case definition reported contact with healthcare services as a result of self-reported symptoms; the most frequently reported contact was a visit to the pharmacy the proportion of participants meeting the ILI case definition slightly decreased compared with the previous week (5.1% compared with 5.3% in week 44) data submitted in the current week (commencing 9 November) is available on the UKHSA data dashboard to support more timely insights. For further details see the data quality report Syndromic surveillance collects data from various healthcare sources where presentations are classified by patterns of symptoms compatible with specific infections. In some settings, the syndromic diagnosis can be supplemented by (rapid) testing. In this report, ED attendances are displayed. Further details and data from other syndromic surveillance systems can be found in the syndromic surveillance weekly summaries . During the week ending on 8 November 2026, ED attendances for acute respiratory infections increased slightly and were similar to the seasonally expected level. ED attendances for COVID-19-like illness decreased. ED attendance for influenza-like illness increased slightly and was above seasonally expected levels. ED attendances for acute bronchiolitis (a syndrome related to RSV infection) increased and were below seasonally expected levels. NHS 111 online assessments for acute respiratory infections increased and remained similar to expected levels, while NHS 111 triaged calls for acute respiratory infections remained stable and below expected levels. GP in-hours consultation rates for lower respiratory tract infections and bronchiolitis increased in children aged 0 to 4 years. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

设拉子住院流感 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 分离株缺乏主要 抗病毒药物 耐药性 突变

Hospitalized influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 isolates in Shiraz lack major antiviral resistance mutations
新闻简报一项于2023年11月至2024年12月在伊朗设拉子进行的研究分析了住院患者中的流感 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 分离株。研究人员收集了300份呼吸道样本,并确定了14例阳性病例以进行进一步的分子分析。调查发现神经氨酸酶基因中存在多种氨基酸替换,包括V241I和N369K,这些替换在92.9%的分离株中普遍存在。然而,这些分离株中均未发现主要耐药性标记,如H275Y或I223V,这些标记已知会降低对抗病毒药物药物如奥司他韦和扎那米韦的敏感性。该研究还通过结构建模和分子对接确认了所分析的变异体仍能预测性地结合这些治疗药物。这些结果表明,在研究期间,标准抗病毒药物治疗方案对设拉子地区流行的流感 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 株仍有效。该研究强调了持续进行基因组监测的重要性,以跟踪可能影响药物疗效的病毒特性变化。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2023年11月至2024年12月进行,报告于2026年11月15日发布(N1)
地点与对象
该研究聚焦于伊朗设拉子的住院患者,共收集了300份呼吸道样本(N9)
事件
一项观察性研究调查了2023年11月至2024年12月期间在伊朗设拉子住院患者中的流感 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 分离株,揭示了基因组变异但未发现主要抗病毒药物耐药性(N1)
规模、影响与风险
该研究从300份样本中鉴定出14种流感 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 分离株,在神经氨酸酶基因中发现了18种不同的氨基酸替换。这些分离株均未携带如H275Y或I223V等主要耐药性标记(N11,N16,N22)
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究通过全长神经氨酸酶基因测序、系统发育分析、结构建模和分子对接评估了抗病毒药物的敏感性。研究结果表明,标准抗病毒药物治疗方案对所分析的分离株仍有效(N12,N13,N24)
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Hospitalized influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 isolates in Shiraz lack major antiviral resistance mutations

News Brief
A study conducted between November 2023 and December 2024 in Shiraz, Iran, analyzed influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 isolates from hospitalized patients. Researchers collected 300 respiratory samples and identified 14 positive cases for further molecular analysis. The investigation revealed multiple amino acid substitutions in the neuraminidase gene, including V241I and N369K, which were prevalent in 92.9% of isolates. However, none of the isolates carried major resistance markers such as H275Y or I223V, which are known to reduce susceptibility to antiviral drugs like oseltamivir and zanamivir. The study also performed structural modeling and molecular docking to confirm that the analyzed variants maintain predicted binding to these therapeutic agents. These findings suggest that standard antiviral treatments remain effective against the circulating influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 strains in Shiraz during the study period. The research highlights the importance of continuous molecular surveillance to monitor potential changes in viral properties that could affect drug efficacy.

HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Tuesday, November 17, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Hospitalized influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 isolates in Shiraz lack major antiviral resistance mutations by November 15, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 4 mins read 0 Hospitalized influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 isolates in Shiraz lack major antiviral resistance mutations Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram An observational study investigating influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 viruses recovered from hospitalized patients in Shiraz, Iran, between November 2023 and December 2024 revealed ongoing genomic variability in the viral neuraminidase gene but found no evidence of established resistance-associated mutations to standard neuraminidase inhibitor therapeutics. Researchers evaluated respiratory specimens from individuals presenting with acute respiratory infections to track how the circulating pathogen has evolved relative to the founding pandemic lineage. The investigation focused on determining whether mutations in the neuraminidase surface glycoprotein might compromise the inhibitory effectiveness of widely utilized antiviral drugs, including oseltamivir and zanamivir. Although the sequenced isolates displayed multiple amino acid substitutions across the neuraminidase gene, none carried the major recognized genetic markers that confer reduced drug sensitivity. The study contextualizes these genomic observations within the broader evolutionary history of the pathogen, noting that the 2009 influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 pandemic caused substantial morbidity and mortality on a global scale. In the years following its initial emergence, continuous molecular surveillance has remained essential because structural changes in the viral neuraminidase gene can alter multiple biological properties. Specifically, amino acid mutations within this gene may influence the ongoing evolution of the virus, facilitate immune evasion against host defenses, and modify viral susceptibility to neuraminidase inhibitors. To assess the molecular characteristics of circulating strains during the 2023–2024 season, the authors conducted an observational surveillance protocol involving patients admitted with acute respiratory infections in Shiraz. Between November 2023 and December 2024, the team collected a total of 300 respiratory samples across the hospitalized cohort. Each specimen was systematically screened for the presence of influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 using real-time reverse transcription polymerase chain reaction assays. From this initial diagnostic screening of 300 clinical samples, the researchers identified fourteen specimens that tested positive for influenza A(H1N1)pdm09, providing the defined sample set for comprehensive downstream molecular and computational characterization. The fourteen confirmed positive isolates subsequently underwent full-length neuraminidase gene sequencing to identify all coding changes present across the complete viral gene. In addition to amino acid substitution analysis, the researchers conducted phylogenetic analysis, structural modeling, and computational molecular docking to evaluate how the observed sequence variations might affect viral protein structure and drug interactions. For phylogenetic comparison, the investigators utilized the historical pandemic reference strain designated A/California/07/2009 (GenBank accession NC_026434.1). Comparing the newly isolated Iranian sequences directly against this reference strain allowed the authors to measure the extent of neuraminidase sequence divergence that has accumulated relative to the founding A(H1N1)pdm09 lineage. Detailed sequence analysis of the fourteen isolates uncovered eighteen distinct amino acid substitutions distributed across the neuraminidase gene. The two most prevalent substitutions detected in the cohort were V241I and N369K, each occurring in 13 of the 14 isolates, representing a frequency of 92.9 percent. The researchers noted that V241I and N369K are consistent with previously documented lineage-associated permissive substitutions that commonly circulate in modern A(H1N1)pdm09 viruses. Among the other identified changes, the N200S substitution appeared in 4 of the 14 isolates (28.6 percent), while the V394I substitution was observed in 2 of the 14 isolates (14.3 percent). Additionally, the analysis identified the N386K substitution, which has been previously associated with the alteration of an N-linked glycosylation site on the neuraminidase protein. A central objective of the genomic evaluation was screening the fourteen neuraminidase sequences for established resistance-associated substitutions recognized by the World Health Organization. This targeted screening revealed that none of the isolates harbored major known neuraminidase inhibitor resistance markers, confirming the complete absence of the classic H275Y substitution as well as the I223V substitution. To supplement the sequence-level screening, the researchers carried out structural modeling and computational molecular docking with the antiviral agents oseltamivir and zanamivir. The docking simulations indicated preserved predicted binding of both oseltamivir and zanamivir to the viral neuraminidase active site, reinforcing the genetic findings that the analyzed viral variants maintain predicted susceptibility to these therapeutic agents. The authors concluded that while influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 viruses isolated from hospitalized patients in Shiraz during the 2023–2024 period exhibited measurable genetic variability in the neuraminidase gene, they completely lacked established neuraminidase inhibitor resistance-associated substitutions. The sequence-based evidence confirms the absence of established resistance markers among the fourteen analyzed clinical isolates, suggesting that standard antiviral therapies retained their molecular targets during the study window. However, because this investigation was strictly observational and did not evaluate clinical trial outcomes or patient treatment responses, the researchers emphasized the necessity of continued molecular surveillance to monitor for the potential emergence of clinically relevant neuraminidase mutations in circulating populations. 季节性流感感染会增加心脏病发作和中风的风险。- Vietnam.vn

Contracting seasonal flu increases the risk of heart attack and stroke. - Vietnam.vn
新闻简报越南新闻网站Vietnam.vn于2026年11月13日发布的一则报道指出,季节性流感感染会增加心脏病发作和中风的风险,尤其是在老年人和患有基础疾病的人群中。越南热带病国家医院和巴莫医院心血管研究所的专家解释称,流感会引发炎症反应,导致血液凝固变化和心血管系统负担加重。报道指出,流感感染后心脏病发作的风险可能增加6-10倍,中风风险可能增加3-10倍。老年人占医院收治病例的70%,占流感相关死亡的85%。一些患者出现了严重的肺炎,需要氧气支持,且治疗已启动。报道强调了主动预防的重要性,包括每年进行一次流感疫苗接种,以减轻这些风险。

地点与对象
越南,老年人群和患有基础疾病的人群。
事件
季节性流感感染会增加心脏病发作和中风的风险,如越南热带病国家医院和巴莫医院心血管研究所专家所报告。
规模、影响与风险
流感感染与心血管事件的显著增加风险相关,尤其是在疾病早期阶段。心脏病发作的风险可能增加6-10倍,而中风的风险可能增加3-10倍。老年人约占医院收治病例的70%,占流感相关死亡的85%。
应对、状态与不确定性
专家指出流感感染的并发症,包括炎症反应、血液凝固增加以及心血管系统负担加重。一些患者出现了需要呼吸支持的严重肺炎,且治疗已实施。对于患有慢性阻塞性肺疾病(COPD)和其他基础疾病的人群,风险尤为突出。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Contracting seasonal flu increases the risk of heart attack and stroke. - Vietnam.vn

News Brief
A news report from Vietnam.vn, published on 2026-11-13, highlights that contracting seasonal flu increases the risk of heart attack and stroke, especially in the elderly and those with underlying health conditions. Experts at the National Hospital for Tropical Diseases and the Cardiovascular Institute, Bach Mai Hospital, explain that influenza triggers an inflammatory response, leading to changes in blood clotting and increased cardiovascular strain. The report notes that the risk of heart attack may rise 6-10 times and stroke risk 3-10 times after flu infection. Elderly individuals account for 70% of hospitalizations and 85% of flu-related deaths. Some patients have developed severe pneumonia requiring oxygen support, and treatment has been initiated. The report emphasizes the importance of proactive prevention, including annual flu vaccination, to mitigate these risks.
Original source text (English)
English Login Homepage Topic News Political System Destination Event Travel Happy Vietnam Enterprise Product Heritage Museum Figure Multimedia Data Home Culture – Society Medical Contracting seasonal flu increases the risk of heart attack and stroke. Seasonal influenza is not simply a respiratory infection; it can also increase the risk of heart attack and stroke, especially in the elderly and those with underlying health conditions. Báo Đầu tư•13/11/2026 Follow Vietnam.vn on Google Add Vietnam.vn on Google 0 Seasonal influenza typically causes fever, cough, sore throat, headache, and fatigue. Most people recover after a period of rest and appropriate treatment. However, for the elderly, those with cardiovascular disease, diabetes, or chronic lung disease, influenza infection can lead to serious, even life-threatening, complications. Get Cold Relief Experts warn that cardiovascular events can occur in the early days of flu infection, even in people who have never been diagnosed with heart disease. According to Associate Professor Dr. Tran Song Giang, Deputy Director of the Cardiovascular Institute, Bach Mai Hospital, when the influenza virus enters the body, an inflammatory response is activated, leading to changes in blood clotting and increasing the burden on the cardiovascular system. Get Cold Relief Notably, the inflammatory response can make atherosclerotic plaques in the arteries less stable and more prone to rupture. This process facilitates the formation of blood clots, causing blockages in blood flow. If this occurs in the coronary arteries, the patient is at risk of myocardial infarction; if it occurs in the cerebral blood vessels, it can lead to ischemic stroke. In addition, influenza infection puts more strain on the heart. When a person has a fever or infection, their heart rate increases, leading to increased oxygen demand from the heart muscle. Conversely, if the flu causes pneumonia or respiratory failure, the amount of oxygen supplied to the body decreases. An imbalance between oxygen demand and supply can increase the risk of myocardial ischemia and worsen pre-existing heart failure. In some cases, direct damage to the myocardium due to the virus may also occur. Associate Professor Tran Song Giang noted that these complications do not only occur in people who have been diagnosed with cardiovascular disease. Even people who have never been diagnosed with heart disease before can still experience complications when infected with influenza. Studies cited by experts show that influenza infection is associated with a significantly increased risk of cardiovascular events, especially in the early stages of the illness. Get Cold Relief Some studies have noted that the risk of heart attack may increase 6-10 times, while the risk of stroke may increase 3-10 times after contracting the flu. These are relative increases in risk observed in specific studies and do not mean that all people with the flu will experience complications. The elderly are a group that requires particular caution. According to data presented at the conference, people aged 65 and older account for approximately 70% of hospitalizations due to seasonal influenza and 85% of influenza-related deaths in the statistics cited. For individuals with underlying conditions such as hypertension, coronary artery disease, heart failure, diabetes, chronic lung disease, or chronic kidney disease, influenza can worsen their health, especially if these conditions are not well controlled. Recently, the National Hospital for Tropical Diseases has been receiving a number of cases of influenza A infection with complications. Notably, some patients have developed severe pneumonia, with lung damage significantly affecting gas exchange capacity, requiring respiratory support with oxygen concentrations of approximately 40-50% to maintain blood oxygenation. Tests revealed an inflammatory response and elevated indicators related to infection. The patient was treated for influenza A, while the infection was controlled and aggressive respiratory support measures were implemented. Get Cold Relief You may also like According to Ms. Le Thi Huyen, from the Intensive Care Unit at the National Hospital for Tropical Diseases, after three days of treatment, the patient's fever has subsided and hemodynamics are relatively stable. However, lung damage remains severe, and the patient is unable to maintain effective respiration independently and must continue non-invasive mechanical ventilation. This development highlights a noteworthy fact: the disappearance of fever does not mean the disease is completely under control. In cases of influenza A complicated by pneumonia, damage to the respiratory system can persist, or even worsen, even if some initial symptoms have subsided. According to doctors, the risk is particularly high for people with chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD). In this group of patients, the airways are already inflamed and obstructed for a long time, impairing ventilation function. Influenza A virus infection can increase inflammation, increase respiratory secretions, and worsen the existing obstruction. Get Cold Relief Simultaneously, lung parenchyma damage caused by the influenza virus can directly affect oxygen exchange capacity, leading to rapid respiratory failure. If not detected and treated promptly, respiratory failure can be life-threatening, especially in the elderly, those with multiple underlying conditions, or those with weakened immune systems. Based on this reality, experts advise people to proactively prevent influenza, especially during transitional seasons and the winter-spring period, when respiratory infections tend to increase. Besides maintaining personal hygiene, washing hands frequently, covering your mouth when coughing or sneezing, keeping your living space well-ventilated, and limiting close contact with people who have symptoms of infection, getting an annual flu vaccine is an important preventive measure. Elderly people and those with cardiovascular disease, heart failure, coronary artery disease, diabetes, chronic lung disease, chronic kidney disease, or immunodeficiency should pay particular attention to vaccination and good management of their underlying conditions. When flu symptoms appear, patients need to closely monitor their health, especially those at high risk. If they experience chest pain, increased shortness of breath, fainting, facial drooping, sudden weakness or paralysis, or difficulty speaking, they must immediately go to a medical facility for emergency treatment and should not self-monitor or treat themselves at home. Get Cold Relief According to experts, seasonal influenza not only affects the respiratory system but can also have serious impacts on the cardiovascular system. Proactive vaccination, managing underlying medical conditions, and early recognition of dangerous signs are crucial factors in reducing the risk of heart attack, stroke, and other life-threatening complications. According to Dr. Nguyen Van My of the Long Chau Vaccination System, the protective efficacy of the flu vaccine under suitable conditions can reach approximately 90%, but the actual level of protection varies depending on the circulating virus strain, age, and health status of the vaccinated individual. The protective ability can also decrease over time, while the flu virus is constantly mutating and new circulating strains are emerging. Therefore, annual flu vaccination is crucial in maintaining protection, especially for young children, the elderly, pregnant women, people with chronic diseases, and those with weakened immune systems. According to Doctor Nguyen Van My, the body usually needs about two weeks after vaccination to develop a protective immune response. Therefore, people should proactively get vaccinated before the flu season or as soon as conditions permit, instead of waiting until the number of cases increases before getting vaccinated. You may also like According to experts, vaccines do not guarantee absolute prevention of all cases of influenza, but they play an important role in reducing the risk of illness and limiting serious consequences. Complete and timely vaccination, including annual booster shots, can contribute to reducing the risk of pneumonia, respiratory failure, hospitalization, and death from influenza. Besides vaccination, the Ministry of Health recommends that people wear masks correctly in healthcare facilities, on public transport, in crowded places, or in enclosed spaces with poor ventilation. People with respiratory symptoms should limit close contact with others and proactively take time off from school or work to avoid infection. Washing hands frequently with soap, covering your mouth and nose when coughing or sneezing, regularly cleaning frequently touched surfaces, and keeping your living space well-ventilated also help reduce the risk of virus transmission. For children with the flu, parents should pay attention to symptoms such as difficulty breathing, rapid breathing, cyanosis, lethargy, seizures, signs of dehydration, or a worsening of the condition after a period of apparent improvement. These are signs that require prompt medical attention and treatment; self-treatment at home should not be prolonged. The Department of Disease Prevention also advises people not to self-medicate with antiviral or antibiotic drugs without a prescription from a healthcare professional. Individuals in high-risk groups should contact a healthcare facility early if they experience suspicious symptoms to receive appropriate advice, assessment, and treatment. 单个氨基酸改变扩大了流感B病毒疫苗的保护范围

Single amino acid change broadens influenza B mRNA vaccine protection
新闻简报一项由北京国家药物控制研究所研究人员于2026年11月14日发表的研究探讨了流感B病毒疫苗保护潜力的增强。该研究聚焦于B/Victoria谱系,其中世界卫生组织推荐的毒株BV21缺乏与广泛交叉保护相关的糖基化位点。通过在BV21的血凝素蛋白中引入D212N 突变突变,研究团队创建了一种改良的疫苗候选疫苗,其在针对B/Victoria谱系多种亚系时表现出增强的交叉中和抗体反应。该研究采用mRNA平台,并在雌性BALB/c小鼠中测试了疫苗,结果显示D212N修饰的疫苗在针对多种流感B病毒株的挑战实验中实现了100%存活率,超过了原始的BV21毒株。研究结果表明,这种最小的抗原修饰可能在不损害其抗原完整性的前提下扩大疫苗的保护范围。未来计划在雪貂模型和人类血清中评估疫苗的性能,以进一步评估其抗原广度及预先存在的免疫对它的影响。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2026年11月14日发表,研究是在加强免疫接种后35天进行的实验。研究结果基于挑战实验后14天内收集的数据。
地点与对象
该研究在北京中国进行,实验对象为雌性BALB/c小鼠。该研究还参考了来自GISAID数据库的全球流感B病毒株。
事件
北京国家药物控制研究所的研究人员证明,对流感B病毒疫苗候选疫苗的血凝素蛋白进行单个氨基酸替换(D212N)显著扩大了其在小鼠中的保护能力。该修饰被证明能够增强对B/Victoria谱系多种亚系的交叉中和抗体反应。
规模、影响与风险
该研究突显了疫苗在针对B/Victoria谱系病毒方面可能的疗效提升,其中D212N修饰的疫苗在挑战实验中实现了100%存活率。世界卫生组织推荐的BV21毒株缺乏与更广泛交叉保护相关的糖基化位点,而这种修饰则解决了这一问题。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究使用mRNA平台并在小鼠中进行了测试。研究结果基于伪病毒中和试验和体内挑战实验。该研究正在进行中,未来计划纳入雪貂模型和具有既往流感感染或疫苗接种的个体血清。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Single amino acid change broadens influenza B mRNA vaccine protection

News Brief
A study published on November 14, 2026, by researchers at the National Institutes for Drug Control in Beijing explored a potential enhancement in influenza B vaccine protection. The research focused on the B/Victoria lineage, where the World Health Organization-recommended strain BV21 lacks a glycosylation position linked to broader cross-protection. By introducing a D212N mutation into the hemagglutinin protein of BV21, the team created a modified vaccine candidate that demonstrated improved cross-neutralizing antibody responses against diverse subclades of the B/Victoria lineage. The study used an mRNA platform and tested the vaccines in female BALB/c mice, with results showing that the D212N-modified vaccine achieved 100% survival in challenge experiments against multiple influenza B strains, surpassing the original BV21 strain. The findings suggest that this minimal antigenic modification could broaden vaccine protection without compromising its antigenic integrity. Future studies are planned to assess the vaccine's performance in ferret models and with human sera to evaluate its antigenic breadth and the influence of pre-existing immunity.
Original source text (English)
