Published in Frontiers in Microbiology, the study details how introducing a D212N mutation into the B/Austria/1359417/2021 (BV21) strain restores a specific glycosylation motif, thereby enhancing cross-neutralizing antibody responses against diverse subclades of the B/Victoria lineage. This minimal antigenic modification addresses a critical limitation in current seasonal influenza B vaccination, where the WHO-recommended BV21 strain lacks a glycosylation position that is associated with broader cross-protection. The study was motivated by the observation that no naturally circulating B/Yamagata-lineage viruses have been confirmed since March 2020, making protection against the B/Victoria (BV) lineage the primary focus of influenza B vaccine development. Previous work by the same team identified that the T214A mutation in the hemagglutinin protein significantly alters cross-neutralization capacity, potentially through changes in glycosylation patterns. However, the BV21 strain, which is currently recommended by the World Health Organization, possesses a threonine at position 214 but differs from the broadly reactive B/Wyoming/06/2014 (BW06) strain at position 212, where it contains an aspartic acid (D) instead of an asparagine (N). The research team hypothesized that this D212N difference might influence glycosylation and, consequently, the breadth of the antibody response elicited by the vaccine. To test this hypothesis, the researchers utilized an mRNA platform to compare the cross-subclade protection provided by the BW06 and BV21 strains within the B/Victoria lineage. They synthesized nucleotide-modified mRNAs encoding the hemagglutinin proteins of these strains and encapsulated them in lipid nanoparticles (LNPs) using an SM-103-based lipid formulation. The team also introduced the D212N substitution into the BV21 background to create a modified vaccine candidate. Quality control of the mRNA-LNP formulations, including particle size, polydispersity index, and encapsulation efficiency, was assessed to ensure consistency across the experimental groups. The immunogenicity of these vaccines was evaluated in female BALB/c mice, which were randomly assigned to vaccine and control groups. The mice received intramuscular injections of the mRNA vaccines, followed by a booster dose after 21 days. Serum samples collected after the booster immunization were tested in pseudovirus neutralization assays to measure cross-neutralizing activity against representative influenza B strains from distinct subclades in the GISAID database. The results showed that while BV21 induced robust neutralization against its homologous V1A.3a.2 subclade, it exhibited weaker activity against other subclades within the B/Victoria lineage. In contrast, the BW06 strain consistently outperformed BV21 in neutralizing heterologous subclades, highlighting its broader and more balanced cross-protective potential. The introduction of the D212N substitution into the BV21 background resulted in a marked increase in neutralizing antibody titers against heterologous strains. Mass spectrometry analysis confirmed the presence of glycosylation at the D212N mutation position, supporting the hypothesis that the restored glycosylation motif contributed to the enhanced immune response. When the ID50 values for heterologous pseudoviruses were normalized to those of the homologous strains, the D212N-modified vaccine demonstrated a more balanced and consistent cross-neutralizing profile than the original BV21 strain. This suggests that the minimal antigenic modification effectively broadens the scope of protection without compromising the antigenic integrity of the vaccine. To assess in vivo protective efficacy, the researchers performed viral challenge experiments with multiple influenza B strains, including B/Brisbane/60/2008, B/Washington/02/2019, and B/Austria/1359417/2021. Mice were challenged intranasally with five times the median lethal dose (LD50) of these viruses 35 days after booster immunization. A quadrivalent influenza vaccine (QIV) was included as a reference control to benchmark the performance of the mRNA vaccines. Body weight and survival were monitored for 14 days following the challenge, with mice that lost more than 25% of their initial body weight being humanely euthanized. The challenge experiments revealed significant differences in protective efficacy among the vaccine groups. Following challenge with B/Brisbane/2008, all mice in the negative control group succumbed by day 6, whereas both low- and high-dose BW06 and BV21 groups exhibited 100% survival. However, when challenged with B/Washington/02/2019, the original BV21 groups showed only 62.5% survival, while the high-dose BW06 group achieved 100% survival. The D212N-modified vaccine demonstrated superior protection in this scenario, with the high-dose group achieving 100% survival, surpassing the original BV21. Across all tested challenge strains, high-dose D212N immunization resulted in 100% survival, indicating a substantial improvement in cross-lineage protection. Pulmonary viral loads and histopathological changes were also evaluated to further characterize the vaccine-mediated protection. On day 4 or 8 post-challenge, lung tissues were collected and analyzed for viral RNA levels using RT-PCR and for pathological changes using hematoxylin and eosin staining. The negative control groups consistently showed significantly higher viral loads and more severe histopathological changes compared to the vaccinated groups. These findings are consistent with the observed protection in terms of body weight maintenance and survival rates. The study also investigated the temporal distribution of amino acid residues at position 212 in influenza B virus hemagglutinin sequences deposited in the GISAID database through 2026. The analysis revealed that the N212 residue was highly prevalent among earlier circulating strains but declined markedly after 2020, accompanied by an increase in D212 and subsequently E212 variants. Notably, despite the predominance of E212 during 2022–2024, the proportion of N212 increased again after 2024, indicating its renewed representation among recently sampled strains. This dynamic variation at HA position 212, which is located within the 190-helix, a major antigenic region of the hemagglutinin protein, underscores the continued relevance of N212 in the evolutionary landscape of influenza B viruses. Despite the promising results, the authors acknowledge several limitations in the study. The evaluation of vaccine immunogenicity was conducted at matched mRNA doses, and cell-based assays were used to confirm antigen expression, but these comparisons do not isolate the potential contributions of differences in antigen abundance or trimer stability. Furthermore, because the D212N substitution changes both the amino acid side chain and the glycosylation status, the specific contribution of the added glycan to the enhanced breadth remains unresolved. The study also did not determine whether the increased breadth involved altered epitope targeting, and the potential involvement of germinal center competition or mannose-binding lectin-mediated antigen clearance remains hypothetical. The findings provide valuable insights for the development of broadly protective influenza B vaccines, particularly in the context of the ongoing threat posed by the rapid antigenic evolution of influenza viruses. The WHO annually recommends vaccine strains for the Southern and Northern Hemispheres, but vaccine-strain mismatches are common due to this rapid evolution. The ability to rapidly adapt mRNA vaccine production to emerging viral strains offers a significant advantage over traditional peptide- or DNA-based platforms. The D212N-modified BV21 mRNA vaccine represents a step toward next-generation vaccines that can provide broader cross-protection against diverse influenza variants. Future studies incorporating ferret models and sera from individuals with prior influenza infection or vaccination could further assess the antigenic breadth and the influence of pre-existing immunity on the vaccine response. The renewed increase in the proportion of N212 after 2024 highlights the importance of monitoring the prevalence of viral strains and adapting vaccine strategies accordingly. These findings support further exploration of glycosylation-site engineering as a strategy to broaden influenza B vaccine protection, potentially leading to more effective and durable seasonal influenza vaccines in the future. 泰兰加纳公共卫生署发布季节性流感预警,气温即将下降 - 泰兰加纳今日

Telangana DPH issues seasonal flu advisory as temperatures set to drop - Telangana Today
新闻简报泰兰加纳公共卫生署署长于2026年11月19日星期四发布了一项季节性流感预警,敦促公众采取预防措施,防止季节性流感和急性呼吸道感染的传播,因为气温预计将在未来几天下降2到3摄氏度。该预警指出,季节性流感是一种急性且具有传染性的呼吸道感染,通过咳嗽和打喷嚏容易传播。易感人群,包括孕妇、5岁以下儿童、老年人以及患有慢性疾病的人,面临更高的严重疾病风险。公共卫生署建议保持卫生、避免拥挤场所,并在出现高烧、呼吸困难和行为异常等警示症状时立即寻求医疗帮助。该预警旨在为公众提供健康指导,以降低气温下降期间流感传播的风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该预警于2026年11月19日星期四发布,因为预计未来几天气温将下降2到3摄氏度。发布日期为2026年11月12日。
地点与对象
该预警由泰兰加纳公共卫生署(DPH)发布,适用于泰兰加纳州的全体人口,特别是易感人群,如孕妇、5岁以下儿童、老年人以及患有慢性疾病的人。
事件
泰兰加纳公共卫生署发布季节性流感预警,敦促公众采取预防措施,防止季节性流感和急性呼吸道感染的传播,因为气温预计会下降。
规模、影响与风险
该预警提到,季节性流感是一种急性且具有传染性的呼吸道感染,当人们咳嗽或打喷嚏时容易在人与人之间传播。易感人群面临更高的严重疾病风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
该预警列出了建议措施,如咳嗽或打喷嚏时遮住口鼻、经常洗手,以及在出现症状时避免前往公共场所。它还强调了在出现警示症状时寻求医疗帮助的重要性。

Telangana DPH issues seasonal flu advisory as temperatures set to drop - Telangana Today

News Brief
The Telangana Director of Public Health issued a seasonal flu advisory on Thursday, November 19, 2026, urging the public to take precautions against the spread of seasonal flu and acute respiratory infections as temperatures are expected to drop by 2 to 3 degrees Celsius. The advisory highlights that seasonal flu is an acute and contagious respiratory infection, spreading easily through coughing and sneezing. Vulnerable groups, including pregnant women, children under five, senior citizens, and individuals with chronic medical conditions, are at higher risk of severe illness. The DPH recommends maintaining hygiene, avoiding crowded places, and seeking immediate medical attention for warning signs such as high fever, difficulty in breathing, and altered behavior. The advisory serves as a public health guidance to mitigate the risk of flu transmission during the temperature drop.

NRI View Point cartoon My Space Education Today Reviews Property Lifestyle E-Paper NRI View Point cartoon My Space Reviews Education Today Property Lifestyle Home | Hyderabad | Telangana Dph Issues Seasonal Flu Advisory As Temperatures Set To Drop Telangana DPH issues seasonal flu advisory as temperatures set to drop The Telangana Director of Public Health has issued a seasonal flu advisory as temperatures are expected to fall by 2 to 3 degrees Celsius. The public has been urged to follow precautions, maintain hygiene and seek medical help for warning signs. By Telangana Today Published Date - 12 November 2026, 11:30 PM Representational Image Hyderabad: The Director of Public Health (DPH), Telangana on Thursday issued an advisory urging the public to take precautions against seasonal flu and spread of acute respiratory infections, as the temperatures are expected to drop by at least 2 degrees Celsius to 3 degrees Celsius in the coming days. Seasonal flu is an acute and contagious respiratory infection characterized by fever, cough, sore throat, body ache and fatigue and it spreads easily between people when they cough or sneeze, the advisory said. People with seasonal flu are advised to rest at home, drink plenty of fluids, and usually recover on their own within a week. The vulnerable groups such as pregnant women, children under 5 years, senior citizens and individuals with chronic medical conditions are at greater risk of severe illness. If they develop seasonal flu symptoms then they are advised to consult a doctor immediately, DPH, Dr Dr.B.Ravinder Nayak said. If individuals having the warning signs such as high fever, difficulty in breathing, blue colour of skin or lips, blood in sputum or altered behavior should, then they should consult the doctor immediately, he added. To Do: Cover mouth and nose with a handkerchief/tissue paper during cough or sneeze. Wash hands often with soap and water. If you have fever, cough and sneezing, avoid stay away from public places Drink plenty of water and eat nutritious food. Adequate ventilation with outdoor air is recommended in all settings to reduce the transmission Don’ts: Avoid shaking hands Avoid reuse of tissue paper or handkerchief Avoid crowded places, frequent touching of eyes, nose & mouth Avoid spitting in public places and self-medicate Join Our Whatsapp Channel Follow Us : Tags acute respiratory infections Director of Public Health Telangana seasonal flu seasonal flu symptoms Related News Telangana issues health advisory on seasonal flu amid drop in temperatures Hyderabad witnesses spurt in seasonal flu, influenza infections Coughing? 流感检测阳性率全国突破10% - 临床实验室产品

Flu Test Positivity Tops 10% Nationwide - Clinical Lab Products
新闻简报The CDC's latest weekly influenza surveillance report for Week 44, ending Nov 7, 2026, indicates rising flu activity across the US, with H1N1pdm09 dominating. Nationally, 11.3% of 52,287 tested specimens were positive for influenza, and influenza A accounted for 99.2% of these cases. Hospitalizations increased to 9,584, with a rate of 2.8 per 100,000 population, and the highest rate was among adults 65 and older at 28.0 per 100,000. Influenza accounted for 0.4% of deaths in Week 44, a figure that has been stable and trending upward for the past eight weeks. The CDC cautioned that an earlier rise in activity does not guarantee a higher seasonal peak or greater severity, and it is too early to determine the implications for vaccine effectiveness. The report also noted that flu test positivity rose in eight of the 10 HHS regions, while it remained stable in Region 8 and declined in Region 9, possibly due to incomplete reporting.
查看新闻五要素
时间
第44周,结束于2026年11月7日;以及从第21周(结束于2026年5月30日)到2026年11月16日。
地点与对象
美国全国范围内,包括所有HHS地区。
事件
全国流感检测阳性率超过10%,主要流行的病毒为流感A型(甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09和甲型H3N2流感病毒)以及流感B型(Victoria和Yamagata谱系)。CDC报告称流感活动和住院病例增加,初步评估显示病情较轻。
规模、影响与风险
Nationally, 11.3% of 52,287 tested specimens were positive for influenza. Influenza A accounted for 99.2% of positive specimens, with H1N1pdm09 being the predominant strain. Hospitalizations increased to 9,584, with a rate of 2.8 per 100,000 population. The highest hospitalization rate was among adults 65 and older at 28.0 per 100,000. Influenza accounted for 0.4% of deaths in Week 44, with a stable figure that has trended upward over the past eight weeks.
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC指出,流感活动的早期上升并不一定导致季节性高峰更高或整体病情更严重。目前尚无法确定哪些病毒可能占主导地位,或者这是否会对疫苗的效果产生影响。CDC还提到,第9地区检测阳性率的下降可能是由于报告不完整所致。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Flu Test Positivity Tops 10% Nationwide - Clinical Lab Products

News Brief
The CDC's latest weekly influenza surveillance report for Week 44, ending Nov 7, 2026, indicates rising flu activity across the US, with H1N1pdm09 dominating. Nationally, 11.3% of 52,287 tested specimens were positive for influenza, and influenza A accounted for 99.2% of these cases. Hospitalizations increased to 9,584, with a rate of 2.8 per 100,000 population, and the highest rate was among adults 65 and older at 28.0 per 100,000. Influenza accounted for 0.4% of deaths in Week 44, a figure that has been stable and trending upward for the past eight weeks. The CDC cautioned that an earlier rise in activity does not guarantee a higher seasonal peak or greater severity, and it is too early to determine the implications for vaccine effectiveness. The report also noted that flu test positivity rose in eight of the 10 HHS regions, while it remained stable in Region 8 and declined in Region 9, possibly due to incomplete reporting.
Original source text (English)

Seasonal influenza activity is increasing in many parts of the US and is higher than typically observed at this time of year, according to the CDC’s latest weekly surveillance report for Week 44, which ended Nov 7. Clinical laboratories nationwide tested 52,287 specimens during the week, and 5,911 (11.3%) were positive for influenza. Flu test positivity rose nationally and in eight of the 10 US Department of Health and Human Services regions. It remained stable in Region 8 and declined in Region 9, which the CDC attributes to possibly incomplete reporting. Since Oct 4 (Week 40), clinical labs have tested 309,131 specimens, with 24,324 (7.9%) testing positive. Influenza A accounted for 99.2% of positive specimens in Week 44. The CDC cautioned that an earlier increase in activity does not necessarily lead to a higher seasonal peak or greater overall severity. Its preliminary in-season assessment classifies severity as low across all age groups, though the agency noted the assessment may change as activity climbs. H1N1 Predominates, With Growing Genetic Diversity Public health laboratories reported 680 positive influenza viruses in Week 44, of which 670 were influenza A and 10 were influenza B. Of the 460 influenza A viruses subtyped, 455 (98.9%) were A(H1N1)pdm09 and 5 (1.1%) were A(H3N2). Since Week 21 (the week ending May 30), 83.4% of reported influenza A viruses have been A(H1N1)pdm09, most of them in the D.3.1.1 HA subclade. The CDC noted that recently circulating D.3.1.1 viruses have divided into distinct groups with different genetic changes in their HA genes, and that there is more genetic diversity among co-circulating A(H1N1)pdm09 viruses than previously seen. The agency said it is too early to know which viruses may predominate or whether there could be implications for vaccine effectiveness this season. Of the 764 influenza viruses genetically characterized since May 24, 555 were A(H1N1)pdm09, and 554 of those (99.8%) belonged to the D.3.1.1 subclade. Among 109 A(H3N2) viruses, 104 (95.4%) were subclade K. The 100 B/Victoria viruses were spread across several subclades, led by C.5.6.1 (48.0%) and C.3.1 (40.0%). Vaccine Match and Antiviral Susceptibility Antigenic testing showed most circulating viruses are well recognized by antisera to the 2026-2027 vaccine reference viruses. Of 147 A(H1N1)pdm09 viruses characterized by hemagglutination inhibition, 140 (95.2%) were well recognized by antisera to the A/Missouri/11/2025-like reference virus. All 35 A(H3N2) viruses and all 33 B/Victoria viruses tested were well recognized by antisera to their respective vaccine reference viruses. For antiviral susceptibility, the CDC tested 758 viruses collected since May 24 against oseltamivir, peramivir, and zanamivir, and 725 against baloxavir. Eight A(H1N1)pdm09 viruses (1.1% of all viruses tested) showed reduced inhibition by oseltamivir and carried the NA-I223V and NA-S247N substitutions. No viruses showed reduced inhibition by peramivir or zanamivir, and none showed decreased susceptibility to baloxavir. Outpatient, Hospital, and Mortality Indicators Outpatient respiratory illness is above baseline. Nationally, 3.2% of ILINet patient visits were for influenza-like illness in Week 44, above the national baseline of 2.9%. Emergency department visits with a discharge diagnosis of influenza reached 2.2%, and the percentage rose in every HHS region except Region 7, where it was stable. The CDC’s ILI activity map shows one jurisdiction at a very high level, six at a high level, and nine at a moderate level. Hospitalization data point to rising severe disease, particularly among older adults. Hospitals reported 9,584 laboratory-confirmed, influenza-associated admissions to the National Healthcare Safety Network in Week 44, a rate of 2.8 per 100,000 population. FluSurv-NET recorded 3,262 laboratory-confirmed influenza hospitalizations between Oct. 4 and Nov. 7, for a cumulative rate of 8.7 per 100,000. The highest rate was among adults 65 and older, at 28.0 per 100,000. Influenza accounted for 0.4% of deaths in Week 44, a stable figure that has trended upward over the past eight weeks. The CDC received reports of two pediatric deaths from the current season this week, bringing the season total to five. One death from the 2025-2026 season was also reported, raising that season’s pediatric death total to 203. The CDC estimates that at least 2.6 million illnesses, 36,000 hospitalizations, and 2,100 deaths have been associated with flu so far this season. Its FluSight forecasts predict a likely national increase in flu hospital admissions, ranging from 8,300 to 34,400, for the week ending Nov. 28. They also predict that 1.8% to 7.7% of emergency department visits will be due to flu. Novel Influenza Surveillance No new confirmed human infections with a novel influenza virus were reported to the CDC as of Nov 7. Public health laboratories have detected four H5-positive specimens since Oct 4, none of them in Week 44. The CDC noted that these figures reflect specimens tested rather than human cases. The CDC continues to recommend annual influenza vaccination for everyone 6 months and older and says prescription antivirals should be started as early as possible, especially for patients at higher risk of complications. All data are preliminary and may change as more reports are received. Week 44, which ended Nov 7, 2026; and since Week 21 (the week ending May 30) up to Nov 16, 2026.
Location and population
Nationwide in the United States, including all HHS regions.
Event
Flu test positivity exceeded 10% nationally, with influenza A (H1N1pdm09 and H3N2) and influenza B (Victoria and Yamagata lineages) being the dominant viruses. The CDC reported increased activity and hospitalizations, with a preliminary assessment of low severity.
Scale, impact and risk
Nationally, 11.3% of 52,287 tested specimens were positive for influenza. Influenza A accounted for 99.2% of positive specimens, with H1N1pdm09 being the predominant strain. Hospitalizations increased to 9,584, with a rate of 2.8 per 100,000 population. The highest hospitalization rate was among adults 65 and older at 28.0 per 100,000. Influenza accounted for 0.4% of deaths in Week 44, with a stable figure that has trended upward over the past eight weeks.
Response, status and uncertainty
The CDC noted that the earlier increase in activity does not necessarily lead to a higher seasonal peak or greater overall severity. It is too early to determine which viruses may predominate or whether there could be implications for vaccine effectiveness this season. The CDC also mentioned that the decline in Region 9 might be due to incomplete reporting.

流感季提前数月开始。一种变异的H1N1毒株可能是原因。

Flu season is months ahead of schedule. A mutated H1N1 strain may be why.

Health officials attribute this early onset to a mutated version of the H1N1 flu strain, which may be better at evading immunity developed from previous infections and vaccines. The CDC is monitoring the situation closely, but wastewater data tracking is experiencing a brief gap due to a new contract with a testing company. The study on the strain's mutations, published on a preprint server, suggests that the virus may be evolving in real time, potentially reducing immunity in some populations. While the CDC has not confirmed the dominance of any specific virus, they are paying extra attention to the mutated H1N1 strain, which is part of the long-circulating seasonal H1N1 lineage.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Flu season is months ahead of schedule. A mutated H1N1 strain may be why.

News Brief
The flu season in the United States has started months earlier than usual, with activity increasing in late August and early September. Health officials attribute this early onset to a mutated version of the H1N1 flu strain, which may be better at evading immunity developed from previous infections and vaccines. The CDC estimates 1.7 million illnesses, 25,000 hospitalizations, and 1,400 deaths from the flu as of October 30, with two new pediatric deaths reported in the last week. Flu activity reached an October level typically seen in December or January, according to wastewater data. The CDC is monitoring the situation closely, but wastewater data tracking is experiencing a brief gap due to a new contract with a testing company. The study on the strain's mutations, published on a preprint server, suggests that the virus may be evolving in real time, potentially reducing immunity in some populations. While the CDC has not confirmed the dominance of any specific virus, they are paying extra attention to the mutated H1N1 strain, which is part of the long-circulating seasonal H1N1 lineage.
Original source text (English)
Find us on Google 📌 America's birthday 🎂 Start the day smarter ☀️ Get the USA TODAY app U.S. Politics Sports Entertainment Life Money Travel Opinion Crossword ONLY AT USA TODAY: Newsletters For Subscribers Real Estate From the Archives USA TODAY PLAY Crossword Marvel Comics eNewspaper Magazines Investigations Video Podcasts Humankind Advertise Your Business WITNESS (True Crime) Just Curious Best-selling Booklist Top Workplaces Nomination Legals Press Releases Cars Boats Grocery Shopping Holidays OUR PORTFOLIO: Real Estate 10BEST USA TODAY Store Shopping Money Essentials WITNESS (True Crime) Southern Kitchen Home Internet Services HEALTH Centers for Disease Control Add Topic Flu season is months ahead of schedule. A mutated H1N1 strain may be why. Mary Walrath-Holdridge USA TODAY Nov. 15, 2026, 6:01 a.m. ET Hear this story Health officials report an unusually early start to the flu season, with activity increasing in late August and early September. A mutated version of the H1N1 flu strain appears to be driving the early spread of infections. The CDC estimates 1.7 million illnesses, 25,000 hospitalizations, and 1,400 deaths from the flu as of October 30. As Americans head into the heart of sickness season, health officials are keeping a close eye on the flu strains driving an unusually early rise in infections. Flu activity began increasing in parts of the United States in late August and early September, earlier than in recent years, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. While flu season typically starts in earnest around November, data used to track respiratory illnesses – like laboratory tests, hospitalizations and wastewater data – show that the season was already well underway before the clock struck Nov. 1. A new strain, a mutated version of H1N1, appears to be driving the spread, with some experts warning that it could play a hand in an earlier or longer season. Here's what that means for this winter's flu season and what experts say people should know. How many flu cases were reported nationwide? Flu cases began to take off in early fall this year, reaching an activity level in October not usually seen until December or January, according to wastewater data. "Seasonal flu activity continues to increase in many parts of the country and is higher than typically observed at this time of year," the CDC said on its influenza tracking site, FluView. The agency estimates 1.7 million illnesses, 25,000 hospitalizations and 1,400 deaths from flu so far this season as of Oct. 30. Two new pediatric deaths were also reported to the agency in the last week. As of Nov. 5, CDC projections found that flu infections were on the rise in 40 states and declining in none. The highest activity seems to be in Southern states and on the West Coast. Flu activity detected from wastewater was still categorized as "very low" nationwide as of Oct. 30, also according to the CDC. It noted that wastewater data tracking is experiencing a "brief gap" in updates due to a new contract with a testing company. It did not clarify how long this gap would last. Additional CDC data through Oct. 31 also showed that about 9.14% (4,694 ) of the 51,351 flu tests administered that week were reported positive to the National Respiratory and Enteric Virus Surveillance System. The vast majority of these confirmed illnesses (97%) were influenza A, the most common form of the flu. Wastewater data maintained by WasteWaterSCAN, a Stanford University-led tool for tracking the spread of disease, categorized flu activity as "high" nationally, meaning higher concentrations of the virus have been detected in the wastewater of more communities nationwide. The same data shows the earliest onset of flu in at least the last four years; samples positive for influenza A were at 71% in October, compared with 40% in September and just 20% in October 2025. The CDC likewise noted that seasonal flu activity began increasing around late August to early September Map of flu activity by state What are the new flu strains? The CDC said "it was too early to know what viruses may predominate" in late October, though scientists and health officials have signaled that they are paying extra attention to a few strains. This doesn't mean this year's viruses have transformed into something dramatically more dangerous or deadly. Rather, mutations have likely made them a little better at slipping past immunity people build from previous infections and vaccines. H1N1, a type of influenza A, is the primary diagnosis behind this year's flu season thus far. You may recognize the name from the "swine flu" pandemic of 2009, caused by a novel strain called H1N1/09. Health officials noted that this year's virus remains part of the long-circulating seasonal H1N1 lineage rather than a new pandemic strain. The most common H1N1 strain currently is the genetic subgroup known as subclade D.3.1.1. Scientists are watching the strain because it has accumulated additional mutations in parts of the virus targeted by human antibodies, raising concerns that some newer versions may be building upon mutations and drifting away from existing immunity almost "in real time," according to a recent study. The study was published on the preprint server bioRxiv, meaning the research has not yet completed the peer-review process. "Why we think we're seeing this now, nobody really knows, but likely it's due to mutations," Dr. Corey K. Smith, emergency physician and Regional Medical Director at Teladoc Health, told USA TODAY. "And what happens is we were all exposed to H1N1 back in 2009, and after some time, as it mutates - and even though we developed this shield, this armor - we have slightly decreased immunity to it." Notably, early evidence shows D.3.1.1 may impact groups of people differently. Some people's antibodies appear to still "recognize" the strain pretty well, while others seem to lose protection. In the September study, researchers noted that teenagers and younger adults seemed to be more affected than adults, specifically people between 15 and 25. This may be thanks to something called "immune imprinting" - a phenomenon in which the body "remembers" the first time it encountered a pathogen and shapes every reaction for the rest of your life based at least partially on this first encounter. So, the first flu strain you were exposed to as a child is the basis for how your immune system responds now. This is why we may see some strains of common illness impacting groups differently along generational lines. In this specific case, the researchers believe that people who were likely first exposed to H1N1 during or shortly after the 2009 pandemic were more affected by D.3.1.1 than people who caught the older strain of H1N1 before 2009. "This is pretty much how viruses work. These viruses, they sit back and say, 'hey, let's attack people, let's attack animals, let's attack birds,' whatever," said Smith. "But as they encounter us and our antibodies, they kind of go back to their homes and say, 'Hey, let's mutate and see if we can come back bigger and stronger next year or next season'." The doctors and scientists who formulate next year's flu shot review these patterns and try to make predictions on how to prevent the next wave, he continued. What are the latest flu symptoms? Symptoms associated with D.3.1.1 are the same as other influenza viruses and include: Fever Body aches and chills Cough Runny or stuffy nose and sneezing Sore throat Headache Fatigue Smith noted that patients often wonder which signs or symptoms can help them figure out whether they have the flu or another respiratory illness, like COVID. One major distinction he noted is the presence or absence of fever and the sniffles. "The symptoms initially start as being relatively similar as you cross different viruses: cough, congestion, malaise (feeling generally unwell), weakness, body aches and a fever," meaning a temperature of 100.4 or higher, he said. Fever, especially in adults, is a strong indicator of flu, along with the sudden onset of body aches and chills. If you felt fine earlier and suddenly "feel like you got hit by a Mack truck" an hour or two later, it is more likely the flu, he explained. "With a common cold, you may have a cough and a runny nose, but you don't have those extreme body aches and head pain," Smith also said. "With COVID, you can have a fever or a little cough, but congestion tends to be the main thing, plus or minus headache and anosmia (loss of taste and smell)." How to protect yourself from the flu You may be tired of hearing it, but the old cliché is true: Washing your hands well – and often – is one of the best ways to keep contagious illnesses at bay, according to the CDC. This applies to all the ailments above and others that often circulate during the wintertime, like respiratory syncytial virus, or RSV. Health agencies recommend washing your hands with hot, soapy water for at least 20 seconds. It's also important to wash them before and after eating, before and after handling food, after using the bathroom and after being around sick people or touching surfaces that could be contaminated. Mask-wearing while sick or out and about during illness season can also help prevent the spread. This is especially important for people at risk of more serious disease, including children and older adults, pregnant people, immunocompromised people and those with underlying conditions like obesity and diabetes. The most effective way to prevent serious illness from the flu is vaccination, according to the World Health Organization, CDC and other prominent medical institutions. Earlier this year, leading medical groups came together to release vaccine recommendations as federal agencies continue to share inconsistent information amid a guideline shakeup and legal challenges under the leadership of known vaccine skeptic and health secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr. The guide recommends the yearly influenza shot to everyone aged six months or older (including pregnant people), with a few exceptions for people with certain medical conditions. People 65+ should recieve a higher dose, if possible. Even if it doesn't stop you from catching the flu entirely, vaccination has still been shown to reduce the severity and length of illness. "That's what vaccines do. It creates a stronger immune response and bigger, stronger antibodies. It gives us a little preview, like a film, like a trailer of what's about to come," said Smith. "Please, get vaccinated. It's not too late and it does contain various mutations and strains of the H1N1, including those that are circulating this year." Featured Weekly Ad

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Be vigilant about the flu during pregnancy to protect the health of both mother and baby. - Vietnam.vn

News Brief
The article published on November 17, 2026, by Vietnam.vn highlights the risks of influenza during pregnancy, emphasizing that it can lead to severe complications such as viral pneumonia, bacterial superinfection, and acute respiratory failure. It warns that symptoms like prolonged high fever, dehydration, and electrolyte imbalance can affect the mother's health and the fetus. The article advises pregnant women to seek medical attention promptly if they suspect influenza and to get vaccinated if there are no contraindications. It also recommends wearing masks, maintaining hygiene, and avoiding close contact with symptomatic individuals. The content is general guidance and does not report on specific outbreaks or confirmed cases, focusing instead on preventive measures and awareness for pregnant women in Vietnam, particularly during transitional seasons and colder months. The article underscores the importance of professional medical supervision and highlights the potential risks of self-medication during pregnancy.
Original source text (English)
Be vigilant about the flu during pregnancy to protect the health of both mother and baby. VTV.vn - Pregnant women who contract influenza are at risk of developing severe symptoms and need to be examined early, treated as prescribed, and proactively vaccinated against the disease. Tìm hiểu lịch sử Đài truyền hình Việt Nam • 16/11/2026 Influenza is a respiratory infection caused by the Influenza virus, circulating year-round and often increasing during transitional seasons. For pregnant women, influenza is not simply a common respiratory illness but can become severe, affecting the health of both mother and fetus. Khám phá thêm Đọc Mẹo Du Lịch Khám phá ĐNA Đọc báo mới According to Dr. Le Van Anh, Deputy Head of the Obstetrics and Gynecology Department at Thuong Tin General Hospital ( Hanoi ), pregnant women with influenza are at risk of developing viral pneumonia, bacterial superinfection, and acute respiratory failure. Prolonged high fever, dehydration, and electrolyte imbalance can also affect the mother's health. Đọc Tin Tức Common symptoms include fever, chills, shivering, cough, sore throat, sneezing, runny nose, nasal congestion, headache, muscle aches, joint pain, and fatigue. Pregnant women should pay particular attention to warning signs such as shortness of breath, rapid breathing, chest pain, cyanosis of the lips or extremities, persistent high fever, lethargy, dizziness, and frequent vomiting. In addition, symptoms such as uterine contractions, abdominal pain, abnormal vaginal bleeding or discharge, and decreased or absent fetal movement should also be checked immediately at a medical facility. You may also like Take proactive measures to prevent seasonal flu when the weather changes. VTV.vn - With changing weather conditions causing a tendency for seasonal flu to increase, parents need to proactively vaccinate their children, maintain hygiene, and monitor for signs of illness. Influenza A cases are on the rise - don't be complacent! VTV.vn - Influenza A is on the rise and can cause serious complications, especially in young children and those at high risk. Take proactive measures to prevent seasonal influenza and reduce the risk of infection in the community. Seasonal influenza is an acute infectious disease caused by the influenza virus, circulating year-round in Vietnam. The number of cases may increase at certain times of the year, depending on the region and weather conditions, especially during transitional seasons and the colder months of autumn and winter. Dr. Le Van Anh noted that self-medication during pregnancy carries many risks. Fever reducers, painkillers, antibiotics, or antiviral drugs used without proper prescription may not be suitable for pregnant women. If a pregnant woman suspects she has the flu, she should go to a medical facility for diagnosis, assessment of the severity of the illness, and treatment advice. Antiviral drugs, if prescribed, should be carefully considered and used according to a doctor's instructions; do not buy medication or treat yourself at home without medical supervision. Regarding prevention, influenza vaccination is an important measure to protect pregnant women, creating antibodies that are passed from mother to child, helping to protect the baby in the first few months of life. Dr. Le Van Anh recommends that pregnant women should be advised to get the influenza vaccine if there are no contraindications. Vaccination should be carried out at a qualified medical facility, after screening and consultation. Besides vaccination, pregnant women should wear masks when going to crowded places, medical facilities, and public transportation; limit close contact with people who have symptoms such as coughing, fever, or sneezing. They should also wash their hands frequently with soap or disinfectant, maintain respiratory hygiene, and keep their living environment well-ventilated. You may also like A balanced diet, adequate rest, and regular prenatal checkups also contribute to protecting the health of both mother and baby. If symptoms suggestive of the flu or any other unusual signs appear, pregnant women should seek medical attention promptly and not delay examination and treatment. 利河谷地区已出现流感病例,流感季提前开始

Lehigh Valley already seeing flu cases as season starts early
新闻简报The article reports that the 2026-27 flu season has started earlier than usual, with hospitals in the Lehigh Valley, Pennsylvania, already seeing flu cases. Dr. Jahre and Dr. Benjamin note that the early season is due to cases rising in the western United States and Pennsylvania. As of November 7, 2026, Pennsylvania had 4,119 influenza cases, with 573 hospitalizations and 8 deaths. Lehigh Valley Health Network (LVHN) reported 766 cases from September 24 to November 7. The flu vaccine is described as highly protective against common strains and mutated strains, though only 40% effective at preventing the flu and 90%-95% effective at preventing moderate and severe symptoms. Officials are urging people to get vaccinated earlier than usual, as the vaccine is a good match for current strains. LVHN held drive-thru vaccination clinics, distributing 2,470 shots at Dorney Park and 1,859 at Hecktown Oaks. The situation remains under investigation, with no indication of resolution yet.
查看新闻五要素
时间
The 2026-27 flu season has started earlier than normal, with cases rising in the western United States and Pennsylvania by November 7, 2026. The article was published on November 16, 2026.
地点与对象
宾夕法尼亚州利河谷地区,病例报告来自利河谷卫生网络(LVHN)和该地区的医院。受影响的人群包括成年人和儿科患者,特别提到18-50岁成年人的疫苗接种率(疫苗接种)较低。
事件
根据雅赫博士的说法,利河谷地区的医院已出现流感病例,因为美国西部地区和宾夕法尼亚州的流感病例提前上升。该事件已由医疗专业人员和卫生网络数据确认。
规模、影响与风险
截至2026年11月7日,宾夕法尼亚州报告了4,119例流感病例,其中573例需要住院治疗,8例死亡。LVHN报告称,从9月24日到11月7日,共有766例病例。该流感疫苗对预防中度和重度症状有效率为90%-95%,但对预防流感整体有效率仅为40%。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生官员鼓励人们比往常更早进行疫苗接种(疫苗接种),因为该疫苗与当前流感病毒株匹配良好。LVHN举办了驱车接种(疫苗接种)诊所,在多尼公园发放了2,470剂疫苗,在赫克敦橡树发放了1,859剂疫苗。目前应对措施仍在进行中,尚未有解决迹象。

The 2026-27 flu season has kicked off a little earlier than normal and hospitals in the Lehigh Valley already are seeing patients Dr. Jeffrey Jahre, St. Luke’s chief emeritus of infectious diseases, said typically flu season doesn’t really take off until after Thanksgiving. However, Jahre said the earliness of this season doesn’t say much else about how the rest of the season will go. “Does it mean that we’re necessarily going to have a worse flu season? The answer to that is no. There really isn’t a good correlation with that at all,” Jahre said. “What it does mean is that we have encouraged people to get an influenza vaccine earlier than what some people might normally feel comfortable with.” Jahre said part of this early season can be attributed to cases picking up early in the western United States. In Pennsylvania, cases are already rocketing upward. On Oct. 24, the state had 1,332 cases of Influenza A and by Nov. 7, cases had risen to 4,119. St. Luke’s identified 766 cases from Sept. 1 to Nov. 7, Jahre said. Lehigh Valley Health Network is also seeing an early rise in cases, according to Dr. Alex Benjamin, chief infection control and prevention officer. It takes about two weeks for immunity from the vaccine to fully build up in the body. This year’s shot is a good match and is highly protective against the worst outcomes of an influenza infection of the most common strains and even mutated strains of influenza. However, Jahre noted that even in a good year, the flu shot is only about 40% effective in preventing the flu. However, it is 90%-95% effective at preventing moderate and severe flu symptoms. The difference between a mild infection and even a moderate flu illness is night and day. A mild case may only have someone out of commission for a day or two. Moderate illness can result in serious fatigue, chills, soreness, weakness, cough, trouble breathing, fever, and congestion, all of which can last for days or weeks. “You can’t use the fact that you come down with the disease even though you get the vaccine as a reason not to take it because ... certainly there is very sound scientific evidence shows that those without any protection are much worse,” Jahre said. As of Oct. 31, 573 Pennsylvanians were hospitalized with flu and eight people had died as part of the current flu season. Jahre said that from Sept. 1 to Nov. 7, St. Luke’s had 73 adult patients and 1 pediatric patient admitted with flu. Jahre said even younger people are not safe and every year the network sees people with the worst outcomes, ranging from being put on ventilators to central nervous system infections, severe pneumonia or cardiomyopathy, a condition where the heart muscles change. But Jahre said yearly flu vaccine uptake has been poor the last few years post-pandemic. He said that among adults ages 18-50, the percentage of vaccinated individuals is as low as 30%. During its recent annual drive-thru clinics, LVHN gave 2,470 flu shots at Dorney Park and 1,859 at Hecktown Oaks. Scale, impact and risk
As of November 7, 2026, Pennsylvania had 4,119 cases of influenza, with 573 hospitalizations and 8 deaths. LVHN reported 766 cases from September 24 to November 7. The flu shot is noted to be 90%-95% effective at preventing moderate and severe symptoms, but only 40% effective at preventing the flu overall.
Response, status and uncertainty
Health officials are encouraging earlier vaccination, as the flu shot is a good match for current strains. LVHN conducted drive-thru vaccination clinics, distributing 2,470 shots at Dorney Park and 1,859 at Hecktown Oaks. The response is ongoing, with no indication of resolution yet.

流感 疫苗接种:流感的最佳防护 - WNEG 电台

Flu Vaccination: The Best Protection Against the Flu - WNEG Radio
新闻简报佐治亚州公共卫生部(DPH)敦促佐治亚州居民现在接种流感疫苗,强调流感 疫苗 是预防该疾病最有效的方法。2026–2027 年的 季节性流感 疫苗 是三价疫苗,可保护三种流感病毒株:甲型 H1N1 流感病毒、甲型 H3N2 流感病毒和乙型 Victoria 流感病毒。DPH 指出,接种 疫苗接种 后大约需要两周时间才能产生抗体,因此早期接种至关重要。流感症状包括发烧、咳嗽、喉咙痛、流鼻涕或鼻塞、头痛、寒战和疲劳,其中突然出现的全身酸痛和不适尤为明显。DPH 建议居民在公共卫生部门、医生办公室、杂货店、社区诊所和药店接种疫苗,并建议采取预防措施,如经常用肥皂和温水彻底洗手,若无法使用肥皂和水,酒精基洗手液是次优选择;咳嗽或打喷嚏时用手肘或手臂遮挡,避免触摸面部,生病时应待在家中。这些措施旨在减少流感及其他呼吸道疾病的传播。如需更多信息,佐治亚州居民可访问 dph.ga.gov/flu 或 CDC 呼吸道病毒防护页面。
查看新闻五要素
时间
佐治亚州公共卫生部(DPH)敦促佐治亚州居民现在接种流感疫苗。流感 疫苗 可在公共卫生部门、医生办公室、杂货店、社区诊所和药店获得。接种 疫苗接种 后大约需要两周时间,身体才能产生抗体并提供对流感的保护,因此现在采取预防措施非常重要。
地点与对象
所有六月龄以上的人都应接种流感 疫苗,极少有例外。
事件
预防流感最有效的方法是接种流感 疫苗。流感是一种可能严重的疾病,尤其是对幼儿和老年人而言,可能导致住院甚至死亡。
规模、影响与风险
所有 2026–2027 年的 季节性流感 疫苗 都是三价疫苗,意味着它们可以保护三种流感病毒株:甲型 H1N1 流感病毒、甲型 H3N2 流感病毒和乙型 Victoria 流感病毒。流感症状包括发烧、咳嗽、喉咙痛、流鼻涕或鼻塞、头痛、寒战和疲劳。其中最显著的流感症状是突然出现的全身酸痛和不适。
应对、状态与不确定性
还有其他经过验证的措施可以帮助预防流感及其他呼吸道疾病的传播,包括:经常且彻底地用肥皂和温水洗手。如果无法使用肥皂和水,酒精基洗手液是次优选择。咳嗽或打喷嚏时用手肘或手臂遮挡,以帮助防止流感传播。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Flu Vaccination: The Best Protection Against the Flu - WNEG Radio

News Brief
The Georgia Department of Public Health (DPH) is urging Georgians to get vaccinated against the flu now, emphasizing that the flu vaccine is the single most effective way to prevent the disease. The 2026–2027 seasonal influenza vaccines are trivalent, protecting against three strains: influenza A (H1N1), influenza A (H3N2), and influenza B/Victoria virus. DPH highlights that it takes about two weeks after vaccination for antibodies to develop, so early vaccination is crucial. Flu symptoms include fever, cough, sore throat, runny or stuffy nose, headache, chills, and fatigue, with a sudden onset of achiness and malaise being particularly notable. The DPH recommends getting vaccinated at public health departments, doctors’ offices, grocery stores, neighborhood clinics, and pharmacies, and also suggests preventive measures such as frequent handwashing, using hand sanitizers, coughing or sneezing into the elbow, avoiding touching the face, and staying home when sick. These measures are aimed at reducing the spread of flu and other respiratory diseases. For more information, Georgians can visit dph.ga.gov/flu or the CDC Respiratory Virus Protection page.
Original source text (English)
Skip to content WNEG The Voice of Wonderful NE Georgia Search for: The Voice of Wonderful NE Georgia Sidebar Menu Home News Obituaries Swap Shop Sports Calendar Listen Live Stephens County Commission Meetings About About WNEG Staff Contact Us Coverage Map Equal Employment Opportunity FCC Public File Tuesday Commentary Archive Pets Auction School Calendar Back to School Programs Flu Vaccination: The Best Protection Against the Flu November 15, 2026 Chad Dorsett By the Georgia Department of Public Health (DPH) The Georgia Department of Public Health (DPH) urges Georgians to get vaccinated against the flu now. With Thanksgiving just two weeks away, you still have time to protect yourself, your family, and your friends. “The single most effective way to prevent the flu is the flu vaccine. Everyone over the age of six months should get a flu vaccine, with rare exceptions,” said Kathleen E.Toomey, commissioner, Georgia Department of Public Health. “It takes about two weeks after vaccination for antibodies to develop in the body and provide protection against the flu, so it’s important to take preventive measures now.” Flu symptoms include fever, cough, sore throat, runny or stuffy nose, headache, chills, and fatigue. One of the most pronounced flu symptoms is a sudden onset of achiness and malaise. Flu is a potentially serious disease, especially for young children and older adults, that can lead to hospitalization and sometimes even death. All 2026–2027 seasonal influenza vaccines are trivalent, meaning they protect against three flu strains: influenza A (H1N1), influenza A (H3N2), and influenza B/Victoria virus. Ask your healthcare provider about what flu vaccine is right for you, as well as COVID and RSV vaccines. Flu vaccines are available at public health departments, doctors’ offices, grocery stores, neighborhood clinics, and pharmacies. There are other tried and true measures you can take to help prevent the spread of flu and other respiratory diseases, including: Wash your hands frequently and thoroughly with soap and warm water. Alcohol-based hand sanitizers are the next best option if you don’t have access to soap and water. Cough or sneeze into the crook of your elbow or arm to help prevent spreading the flu. Avoid touching your face, as flu germs can get into the body through the mucous membranes of the nose, mouth, and eyes. If you are sick, stay home from school or work. For more information about flu and how to prevent it, log on to dph.ga.gov/flu. You can learn more about COVID and RSV vaccines at CDC Respiratory Virus Protection. You can monitor Georgia weekly influenza reports at Georgia Flu Activity. The reports are updated on Fridays. VK Facebook Twitter Google+ News Sidebar

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

儿科住院流感检测与华南地区监测相关性呈正相关

Pediatric Inpatient Influenza Detections Correlated Positively With Southern China Regional Surveillance
新闻简报一项于2026年11月15日发表的研究分析了2021年1月至2026年5月期间,中国南方地区儿科及新生儿住院患者的21,965份咽拭子检测记录。研究发现,包括甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09、甲型H3N2流感病毒和B型在内的流感类型和亚型的月度占比与来自中国国家流感中心的区域监测数据呈正相关。在每个日历年度内,通过医院检测和区域监测数据,均一致识别出相同的主导流感类别。研究还表明,6至14岁儿童感染流感甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09、甲型H3N2流感病毒和B型的调整比值比高于1岁以下婴儿。作者得出结论,常规住院检测可以有效补充区域流感监测,提供本地化、按年龄分层的检测数据。研究结果突显了基于医院的分子检测在与更广泛的流行病学趋势对齐方面的潜力,为住院儿童中季节性流感的动态提供了见解。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究历时65个月进行,发表日期为2026年11月15日。数据收集时间跨度为2021年1月至2026年5月。
地点与对象
该研究聚焦于中国南方地区的儿科住院患者,具体为0至14岁患有下呼吸道感染的儿童。
事件
对儿科住院流感检测记录的回顾性分析显示,流感类型和亚型的月度占比与来自中国国家流感中心的区域监测数据呈正相关。研究还发现,在每个日历年度内,通过医院检测和区域监测数据,均一致识别出相同的主导流感类别。
规模、影响与风险
该研究报告了流感甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09的检测阳性率为2.51%,流感甲型H3N2流感病毒为3.34%,流感B型为2.18%。研究结果表明,常规住院检测可以有效补充区域流感监测,通过提供本地化、按年龄分层的检测数据。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是一项回顾性分析,使用分子检测记录和统计模型。研究显示,医院流感检测与区域监测数据之间存在强正相关性,在44个月内识别主导类别的一致性达到79.5%。作者得出结论,常规住院检测可以有效补充区域流感监测。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Pediatric Inpatient Influenza Detections Correlated Positively With Southern China Regional Surveillance

News Brief
A study published on November 15, 2026, analyzed 21,965 throat-swab testing records from pediatric and neonatal inpatients in southern China between January 2021 and May 2026. The research found that monthly shares of influenza types and subtypes, including A(H1N1)pdm09, A(H3N2), and B, were positively correlated with regional surveillance data from the Chinese National Influenza Center. Within each calendar year, the same leading influenza category was consistently identified through both hospital-based detections and regional surveillance. The study also revealed that children aged 6 to 14 years had higher adjusted odds ratios for influenza A(H1N1)pdm09, A(H3N2), and B compared to infants under 1 year. The authors concluded that routine inpatient testing can effectively supplement regional influenza surveillance by providing localized, age-stratified detection data. The findings highlight the potential of hospital-based molecular testing to align with broader epidemiological trends, offering insights into seasonal influenza dynamics among hospitalized children.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Tuesday, November 17, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Pediatric Inpatient Influenza Detections Correlated Positively With Southern China Regional Surveillance by November 15, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 2 mins read 0 Pediatric Inpatient Influenza Detections Correlated Positively With Southern China Regional Surveillance Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Routine pediatric inpatient molecular test records revealed that monthly shares of influenza types and subtypes were positively correlated with aggregate regional surveillance data from the Chinese National Influenza Center for southern China. In a 65-month retrospective study examining hospital testing records, investigators observed that annual leading influenza categories shifted multiple times over a five-year period, while routine hospital-based detections demonstrated substantial temporal alignment with broader regional surveillance trends. The retrospective evaluation addressed whether routine molecular testing provides clinical and epidemiological value for describing long-term viral dynamics among hospitalized children and whether those hospital-derived patterns mirror regional surveillance. Although routine molecular assays systematically detect influenza types and subtypes in clinical practice, their utility for tracking multi-year patterns in pediatric inpatient populations and their comparability to established regional surveillance networks had remained unclear. To investigate these dynamics, researchers analyzed 21,965 throat-swab testing records collected between January 2021 and May 2026. The dataset comprised 17,835 pediatric and neonatal inpatients aged 0 to 14 years who presented with clinician-recorded lower respiratory tract infections. Across the entire 65-month surveillance period, overall test positivity reached 2.51% for influenza A(H1N1)pdm09, 3.34% for influenza A(H3N2), and 2.18% for influenza B. Between 2021 and 2025, the leading viral category changed several times across the five complete calendar years evaluated. Within each individual calendar year, crude positivity and age-standardized positivity consistently identified the exact same leading influenza category. Multivariable logistic regression models with patient-clustered robust standard errors demonstrated that the strength of age associations differed substantially across influenza categories, supported by a statistically significant interaction between viral category and age group (P < 0.001). When compared with infants under 1 year of age, children aged 6 to 14 years exhibited adjusted odds ratios of 1.58 for A(H1N1)pdm09, 5.94 for A(H3N2), and 4.23 for influenza B. Temporal comparisons between the hospital’s monthly category composition and aggregate surveillance from the Chinese National Influenza Center revealed strong positive correlations across 47 concurrent months, with Pearson r values of 0.765 for A(H1N1)pdm09, 0.662 for A(H3N2), and 0.895 for influenza B. Furthermore, both series identified the same leading category in 35 of the 44 months possessing a unique leading category in both datasets, representing 79.5% agreement with a Cohen’s kappa of 0.693. The authors concluded that routine inpatient testing can effectively supplement regional influenza surveillance by supplying localized, age-stratified detection data across influenza types and subtypes. (2026). Influenza type and subtype dynamics and temporal concordance with regional surveillance: a 65-month retrospective analysis of pediatric inpatient test records. Time
The study was conducted over a 65-month period, with the publication date on November 15, 2026. The data collection spanned from January 2021 to May 2026.
Location and population
The study focused on pediatric inpatients in southern China, specifically children aged 0 to 14 years who presented with lower respiratory tract infections.
Event
A retrospective analysis of pediatric inpatient influenza testing records showed that the monthly shares of influenza types and subtypes were positively correlated with regional surveillance data from the Chinese National Influenza Center. The study also found that within each calendar year, the same leading influenza category was identified through both hospital-based detections and regional surveillance.
Scale, impact and risk
The study reported test positivity rates of 2.51% for influenza A(H1N1)pdm09, 3.34% for influenza A(H3N2), and 2.18% for influenza B. The findings indicate that routine inpatient testing can effectively supplement regional influenza surveillance by providing localized, age-stratified detection data.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study was a retrospective analysis using molecular testing records and statistical models. It demonstrated strong positive correlations between hospital-based influenza detections and regional surveillance data, with 79.5% agreement in identifying the leading category in 44 months. The authors concluded that routine inpatient testing can effectively supplement regional influenza surveillance.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

流感进入活跃期 成都疾控提醒做好流感预防

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报成都市公共卫生部门于2026年11月11日宣布,该市已进入流感活跃季节,流感活动指数达到四级。根据全国监测数据显示,主要流行毒株为甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09、甲型H3N2流感病毒和B/Victoria毒株,官员警告称,气温下降和室内人群聚集将增加感染风险。该通告由成都市疾病预防控制中心和市气象局联合发布,强调流感并非普通感冒,可能导致肺炎,尤其是儿童、老年人和患有慢性疾病的人群。通告呼吁居民接种三价流感疫苗,改善通风,隔离患病家庭成员,并严格遵守卫生习惯。该指导反映了季节性趋势,而非特定暴发,且文章部分内容有限,限制了进一步的详细信息。
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章于2026年11月11日(20:22)发布,并提到2026年11月当前时期是流感活动上升的秋季-冬季过渡期。
地点与对象
该警报涉及中国四川省成都市,针对一般人群,尤其是儿童、老年人和患有慢性疾病的人群,他们面临更高的流感严重并发症风险。
事件
成都市疾病预防控制中心与市气象局联合发布公共卫生通告,指出成都市流感活动指数已达到四级,表明流感季节活跃。该通告为指导性建议,而非确认暴发的报告,强调疫苗接种和基本预防措施。
规模、影响与风险
全国监测数据显示,第44周(10月26日至11月1日)的阳性样本中,65.4%为流感A型(其中51.2%为H1N1pdm09亚型,48.8%为H3N2亚型),34.6%为流感B型(Victoria谱系)。未提供本地病例或死亡人数,但报告指出,随着气温下降和室内人群聚集增加,流感感染风险预计会上升。
应对、状态与不确定性
疾病预防控制中心建议接种三价流感疫苗(覆盖H1N1pdm09、H3N2和B/Victoria毒株),定期通风,隔离有症状的个体,并采取严格的卫生措施。该指导基于季节性趋势,而非特定暴发数据,且来源材料仅部分可用,限制了对任何额外细节的验证。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
流感进入活跃期 成都疾控提醒做好流感预防
News Brief
Chengdu’s public health authorities announced on November 11, 2026 that the city has entered an active influenza season, with the flu activity index reaching level IV. Drawing on national surveillance showing a predominance of A(H1N1)pdm09, A(H3N2) and B/Victoria strains, officials warned that cooler weather and indoor crowding will raise infection risk. The advisory, issued jointly by the Chengdu CDC and the municipal meteorological bureau, stresses that flu is not a simple cold and can lead to pneumonia, especially in children, seniors and those with chronic illnesses. It urges residents to receive the trivalent flu vaccine, improve ventilation, isolate sick household members, and practice strict hygiene. The guidance reflects seasonal trends rather than a specific outbreak, and the article’s partial content limits further detail.
Original source text (Chinese)
流感进入活跃期 成都疾控提醒做好流感预防 锦观新闻 2026-11-11 20:22发布于四川成都日报《锦观新闻》官方账号 问AI · 不同亚型缺乏交叉保护,三价疫苗如何护今冬? 11月进入秋冬交替的关键期,气温下降、室内聚集变多,流感也进入了流行季。近日,成都疾控、成都气象联合发布提示,成都市流感综合指数分级为IV级,提示流感处于活跃期。本月陆续有降温降水过程,流感发病风险将有所增加。 针对当前情况,成都市疾控中心专家提示,流感不是普通感冒,严重时可能引发肺炎等并发症,接种流感疫苗是预防流感最有效的手段。幼儿、老人、慢性病患者等是重症高危人群。 据中国疾控中心第44周(10月26日—11月1日)流感监测周报显示,全国检出流感阳性样本中,甲型流感病毒占比65.4%、乙型流感病毒占比34.6%;甲型流感病毒中A(H1N1)pdm09占比51.2%、A(H3N2)占比48.8%,乙型流感病毒均为B(Victoria)系。据国家疾控中心王大燕介绍,同一个流感流行季中可能有不同型别或亚型共同或交替流行,不同亚型之间缺乏有效的交叉保护,感染过其中一种后,仍有可能再感染其他型别或亚型。今年我国上市的流感疫苗均为三价流感疫苗,针对上述甲型H1N1、甲型H3N2和乙型Victoria系三种流行亚型,均有很好的预防作用。 成都市疾控中心专家王勤俭表示,接种流感疫苗是预防流感最有效手段。香港卫生防护中心分析上一年度数据发现,未接种流感疫苗的儿童在感染流感后出现严重并发症或死亡的比率,是已接种疫苗儿童的约5倍。“这一数据对成都家长同样具有参考意义,目前接种疫苗对今冬明春流感仍具有保护作用。” 而在日常防护方面,王勤俭提醒,居家时,一老一小要重点落实开窗通风,每日2-3次,每次半小时,通风时做好保暖;家人患病后尽量单独居住活动,碗筷水杯分开使用;符合条件的家庭成员尽早完成流感疫苗接种。在幼儿园、学校,孩子出现发烧、咳嗽等异常及时居家休息,不带病返校;水杯、毛巾、文具不共用;教室定时通风,门把手、玩具定期清洁消毒。 流感流行季尽量少带宝宝长时间停留在密闭拥挤的室内场所;去医院等场所规范佩戴口罩;触碰电梯按键、扶手后尽快用流水洗手。孩子发烧咳嗽,重点观察精神状态。一旦出现嗜睡、烦躁不安、抽搐或呼吸急促等异常变化,不要在家硬扛,立即做好防护后就医。 记者 胡瑰玮 图片 成都疾控 责任编辑 王欢 编辑 常莉娟 段祯
View five news elements
Time
The article was published on 2026-11-11 (20:22) and refers to the current November 2026 period as the start of the autumn‐winter transition when influenza activity rises.
Location and population
The alert concerns Chengdu city in Sichuan Province, China, and addresses the general population – especially children, the elderly and people with chronic diseases – who are at higher risk of severe flu complications.
Event
Chengdu Center for Disease Control, together with the municipal meteorological bureau, issued a public health notice that the city’s influenza activity index has reached level IV, indicating an active flu season. The notice is a guidance advisory, not a report of a confirmed outbreak, and it emphasizes vaccination and basic preventive measures.
Scale, impact and risk
National surveillance data for week 44 (26 Oct‐1 Nov) show 65.4% of positive samples are influenza A (with 51.2% H1N1pdm09 and 48.8% H3N2) and 34.6% are influenza B (Victoria lineage). No local case or death counts are provided, but the report states that the risk of flu infection is expected to increase as temperatures fall and indoor crowding rises.
Response, status and uncertainty
The CDC recommends vaccination (trivalent vaccine covering H1N1pdm09, H3N2 and B/Victoria), regular ventilation, isolation of symptomatic individuals, and hygiene practices. The guidance is based on seasonal trends rather than specific outbreak data, and the source material is only partially available, limiting verification of any additional details.

流感不等于普通感冒,二者在发病、表现以及传染性等方面有着明显区别。 普通感冒多由鼻病毒等病原体引发,起病较缓,大多为低热,甚至不发热,主要以鼻塞、流涕、咽痛等上呼吸道局部症状为主,传染性较弱,一般很少出现严重并发症。 而流行性感冒由甲型或乙型流感病毒感染所致,起病突然,常出现39摄氏度以上的高热,伴随头痛、浑身酸痛、显著乏力等不适。流感的传染性强,传播速度快,还容易引发肺炎等严重并发症。 从上述区 ...
